## Document 001: -335亿美元！亚马逊的这个数字，“吓蒙”了市场

Source PDF: all report pdf/-335亿美元！亚马逊的这个数字，“吓蒙”了市场.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

亚⻢逊三季度⾃由现⾦流骤降⾄ -335 亿美元，较市场预期 -77 亿美元超出四倍，背后是单季
773 亿资本开⽀与 481 亿新增债务的双重冲击。 AWS 增速虽创五年新⾼达 39.3% ，却仍低于
买⽅预期，利润率反向下滑。四季度运营利润指引 310 亿，全⾯低于华尔街及买⽅预期。核
⼼症结在于： AWS 每赚 1 美元，背后捆绑近 4 美元新增⽀出。
摘要：
亚⻢逊三季度财报⽤⼀个极其惨烈的数字回答了华尔街全年的疑问：单季⾃由现⾦流消耗⾼达
335 亿美元。尽管 AWS 云业务创下近五年最快增速，但失控的资本⽀出（ Capex ）、疲软的四季
度指引以及暴增的债务，彻底击碎了买⽅的乐观预期。
亚⻢逊最新财报显⽰，三季度⾃由现⾦流录得 -335 亿美元，⽽彭博市场⼀致预期仅为 -77 亿美
元，实际结果超出预期四倍以上。 单季资本开⽀飙升⾄ 773 亿美元，环⽐增⻓ 43% ；为填补资
⾦缺⼝，公司在该季度新增 481 亿美元⻓期债务。滚动 12 个⽉⾃由现⾦流从上季度的 -76 亿美元

急剧恶化⾄ -424 亿美元。
与此同时，四季度业绩指引令市场失望。营收指引中值为 2435 亿美元，略低于市场⼀致预期的
2442 亿美元；运营利润指引中值 310 亿美元，不仅低于华尔街预期的 330 亿美元，更全⾯低于买
⽅层⾯ 350 亿美元的 " ⼼理关⼝ " 。 ⾼盛在盘后快评中指出，多项核⼼指标相对于市场预期呈
现 " 中性偏弱 " 结果，预计股价将出现初步负⾯反应。
财报公布后，亚⻢逊盘后股价⼀度暴跌 12.5% ⾄ 223 美元附近（最低触及约 214 美元），远超
7.5% 的隐含波动率，抹平了⼆季度以来的全部涨幅，回落⾄今年 4 ⽉初⽔平。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

对于投资者⽽⾔，核⼼逻辑已经发⽣根本性转变：亚⻢逊的股票之所以 “ 涨不动 ” ，是因为 AWS
业务每带来 1 美元的潜在收益，背后都捆绑着远超 1 美元（甚⾄接近 4 美元）的新增⽀出。华尔街
的预期游戏已经失效，现在市场必须直⾯⾼昂的账单。
那个关键数字：单季 -335 亿美元
如果说有⼀个数字概括了亚⻢逊当前的处境，那就是-335 亿美元—— 这是公司三季度单季⾃由
现⾦流。
这⼀数字的背后逻辑极为直⽩：单季经营性现⾦流约 424 亿美元，但净资本开⽀⾼达 759 亿美
元，两者之差即为现⾦⿊洞。⽽就在⼀年前，亚⻢逊的⾃由现⾦流还处于 529 亿美元的历史峰
值。
资本开⽀的膨胀速度同样触⽬惊⼼。三季度⽑资本开⽀达 773 亿美元，环⽐⼆季度的 542 亿美元
增⻓ 43% 。亚⻢逊同时确认，预计 2026 年全年资本开⽀约 2200 亿美元的计划不变。按前三季度
已累计净⽀出约 1720 亿美元计算，四季度资本开⽀将约为 480 亿美元，较刚刚完成的三季度骤降
约 37% 。
这⼀测算令投资者⽣疑：要么亚⻢逊将在四季度⼤幅收紧⽀出，与 CEO Andy Jassy 所描述的 " 需

求持续超过供给 " 形成⽭盾；要么三季度存在⼤量⽀出的提前确认，使得原有指引本⾝已失效。
⽆论哪种情形，市场均难以认同 2027 年资本开⽀将低于 2026 年 —— 此前买⽅预期明年资本开⽀
在 3200 亿⾄ 3750 亿美元之间，⽽亚⻢逊在本次财报中对 2027 年的数字只字未提。
谁来买单？债务飙升，资产负债表急剧恶化

### Page 3

当运营现⾦⽆法覆盖⽀出时，钱只能从别处来。亚⻢逊的资产负债表给出了答案：疯狂发债。
为了⽀撑庞⼤的开⽀，亚⻢逊在三季度筹集了 481 亿美元的新增⻓期债务。今年前九个⽉，公司
新增⻓期借款⾼达 1151 亿美元，导致⻓期债务⼏乎翻了三倍，从 2025 年底的 656 亿美元飙升⾄ 9
⽉底的 1731 亿美元。
与此同时，公司的财务健康度正在全⾯下滑：
现⾦储备从 868 亿美元骤降⾄ 558 亿美元。
利息⽀出同⽐翻了三倍多，从去年同期的 5 亿美元飙升⾄ 17 亿美元。
“ 其他⻓期负债 ” 也⼏乎翻了三倍，从 360 亿美元增⾄ 999 亿美元。
值得注意的是，上述数据还不包括亚⻢逊⾸次发⾏的英镑计价债券，以及⽤于购买英伟达芯⽚
的 80 亿美元特殊⽬的载体安排。
正如市场所观察到的逻辑链条： “债务变为资本开⽀，资本开⽀变为营收，营收变为每股收益。 ”
亚⻢逊本季度在规模上完整展⽰了这条链条的第⼀个环节。
AWS 与核⼼业务：五年最佳增速仍难填买⽅胃⼝
尽管 AWS 云业务表现强劲，营收增⻓ 39.3% ⾄ 460 亿美元，创下近五年最快增速，且 CEO Jassy

给出了 2027 和 2028 年 AWS 增⻓ “ 超过 40%” 的乐观预期。但这对挑剔的买⽅来说还不够：39.3%
的增速虽然击败了华尔街约 37% 的预期，但低于买⽅真正期待的 42% 。
更糟糕的是利润率⾛向了错误的⽅向。AWS 营业利润率从⼆季度的 39.4% 下滑⾄ 38.7%，未能
达到买⽅预期的 “40% 出头 ” 。
其他业务⽅⾯，零售表现平稳（北美销售额增⻓ 6% ，国际增⻓ 8% ，剔除汇率影响）。但⼴告业
务增速放缓⾄ 12%（剔除汇率影响），公司将其归咎于 Prime Day 的 “ 时间异常 ” ，并承诺四季度将
反弹⾄⾼⼗位数增⻓。
此外，每股收益（ EPS ）录得 1.89 美元，不及预期的 1.97 美元。这主要是因为去年三季度包含了
Anthropic 股份带来的 95 亿美元税前收益，⽽今年⾮运营项⽬反⽽造成了 12 亿美元的拖累（包含

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

翻倍的利息账单）。唯⼀符合预期的是营业利润，达到创纪录的 281 亿美元（利润率 13.9% ），精
准命中买⽅传⾔的 280 亿美元。
疲软的四季度指引：全⾯不及预期
四季度业绩指引是此次财报的另⼀个失望来源。
营收指引区间为 2400 亿⾄ 2470 亿美元，中值 2435 亿美元，略低于市场⼀致预期的 2442 亿美
元。运营利润指引为 285 亿⾄ 335 亿美元，中值 310 亿美元，不仅低于华尔街预期的 330 亿美元，
也低于买⽅⼼理关⼝的 300 亿⾄ 350 亿美元区间的两端，缺⼝超过 15 亿美元。
据摩根⼤通分析师 Mark Schilsky 此前预测，⼀旦亚⻢逊内部 AI 助⼿ Leo 正式上线、相关成本开始
资本化，运营利润将获得约 10 亿美元的额外提振 —— 即便如此，实际指引仍未能达到预期。

⼴告业务是指引中唯⼀的亮点：三季度⼴告增速放缓⾄ 12% （剔除汇率影响），公司将此归因于
Prime Day 的 “ 时间性异常 ” ，并预计四季度将反弹⾄ ” ⾼⼗字头 ” 增速区间。不过，随着亚⻢逊屏
蔽 Meta Muse 等 AI 购物代理、市场担忧 AI 代理购物将侵蚀赞助商品⼴告收⼊，⼴告业务的⻓期前
景仍存争议。
为什么这只股票 “ 永远涨不动 ” ？
摩根⼤通分析师 Mark Schilsky 在财报前已对亚⻢逊的困境作出精准诊断：
据⾼盛和瑞银的评估，亚⻢逊财报前是科技股中拥挤度最⾼的多头头⼨之⼀，⾼盛 TMT 桌拥挤
度评分 8/10 ，瑞银情绪量表 9/10 。⾼度拥挤的多头持仓意味着，⼀旦现实未能匹配预期，⽌损卖
压将成倍放⼤。
三季度财报的结构性⽭盾清晰可⻅： AWS 加速兑现，但每⼀美元的云业务收⼊，背后跟随着远
超⼀美元的新增⽀出。本季度，这⼀⽐例接近四⽐⼀。  只要这⼀模式未能扭转，股价上涨的逻
辑便始终⾯临来⾃资产负债表的压制。
这张账单是：单季 773 亿美元的资本⽀出、 335 亿美元的现⾦消耗，以及 481 亿美元的新增债

务。亚⻢逊的股票之所以表现挣扎，是因为 AWS 带来的每⼀美元上⾏空间，都捆绑着超过⼀美
元的新增⽀出 —— ⽽最新财报显⽰，这个⽐例接近⼀⽐四。
市场终于意识到，⾼昂的基础设施投⼊正在⽆情地吞噬股东的现⾦流。
“AMZN 是今年最让主流投资者沮丧的股票之⼀ …… 华尔街对 2027 年 AWS 的预测低得可笑 …… 正
因如此，投资者都把它视为容易超预期的故事，但所有⼈都知道这⼀点，这恰恰是它涨不动的核
⼼原因。 ”

## Document 006: 10月PMI数据点评：制造业需求整体回落至收缩区间

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月PMI数据点评：制造业需求整体回落至收缩区间.pdf
Report source: 中银证券
Date: 2026-11-01
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

宏观经济 | 证券研究报告 — 总量点评 2026 年 11 月 1 日

10 月 PMI 数据点评

制造业需求整体回落至收缩区间   
制造业景气度回落至收缩区间。制造业需求整体回落至收缩区间。尽管 10
月建筑业景气度有所回落， 但10 月专用设备制造业长期盈利能力领先于其他
制造业子行业，四季度固定资产投资仍有望发力。 
 制造业景气度回落至收缩区间。10 月制造业 PMI 指数为 49.3%， 环比 （9
月，下同）下降 0.8 个百分点，制造业景气度回落至收缩区间。 
 分产业看，10 月高技术制造业 PMI 为 51.3%，环比下滑 1.2 个百分点，
但仍高于制造业总体水平，且维持在较高景气度区间，仍是当前制造业
景气度回升的重要驱动；原材料行业 PMI 为 47.3%，在收缩区间有所下
探；10 月消费品行业 PMI 为 48.8%，环比下滑 1.9 个百分点，行业景气
度回落至收缩区间，中秋、国庆假期因素消退后，消费行业景气度或出
现自然回落。 
10 月装备制造业 PMI 为 50.6%，较 9 月小幅下滑 0.4 个百分点，但仍然
维持在扩张区间； 尽管10 月建筑业 PMI 再度回落至收缩区间， 但装备制
造业 PMI 仍表现出一定韧性，我们认为，四季度扩内需政策仍有发力空
间，或对相关产业景气度有所带动。 
 制造业需求整体回落至收缩区间。着眼重要细分项， 10 月新订单指数
49.4%，环比下滑 1.1 个百分点；10 月新出口订单指数环比回落 1.3 个百
分点，当月值为 48.7%；10 月产成品库存指数 47.5%， 环比下滑0.1 个百
分点；制造业需求整体回落至收缩区间。 
10 月生产指数 49.8%，环比回落 1.9 个百分点；原材料库存指数 48.4%，
环比上升 0.2 个百分点； 从业人员指数48.4 %， 环比9 月持平； 供货商配
送时间指数 50.2%，环比上升 0.1 个百分点。企业预期方面，制造业生产
经营活动预期指数 53.4%，环比回落 0.4 个百分点，制造业经营预期维持
在较高景气度区间。从生产相关的细分指标看，月内在手订单和采购指
数环比分别下滑 1.9 和 2.1 个百分点。 
10 月制造业生产指数环比下降， 制造业采购备货、 在手订单均环比下降，
或在一定程度上受到假期因素影响，国庆假期时间较长，工作日减少或
对企业生产有所影响。 
 10 月非制造业 PMI 指数回升至扩张区间。10 月非制造业 PMI（商务活动）
指数为 49.7%， 环比下滑0.5 个百分点。 从需求端看，10 月新订单指数为
44.4%，环比下滑 2.1 个百分点。外需方面，新出口订单指数为 46.5%，
环比下滑 2.0 个百分点。10 月非制造业从业人员指数为 46.0%， 环比上月
走高 0.1 个百分点，但仍处于较深的收缩区间。 供应商配送时间指数
50.7%，环比回落 0.6 个百分点。 
 风险提示：海外大宗商品价格震荡；地缘关系的不确定性；政策效果不
及预期。

### Page 2

2026 年 11 月 1 日 10 月 PMI 数据点评 2

制造业需求整体回落至收缩区间 
制造业景气度回落至收缩区间。10 月制造业 PMI 指数为 49.3%，环比（9 月，下同）下降 0.8 个百
分点，制造业景气度回落至收缩区间。 
分产业看，10 月高技术制造业 PMI 为 51.3%，环比下滑 1.2 个百分点，但仍高于制造业总体水平，
且维持在较高景气度区间，仍是当前制造业景气度回升的重要驱动；原材料行业 PMI 为 47.3%，在
收缩区间有所下探；10 月消费品行业 PMI 为 48.8%，环比下滑 1.9 个百分点，行业景气度回落至收
缩区间，中秋、国庆假期因素消退后，消费行业景气度或出现自然回落。 
10 月装备制造业 PMI 为 50.6%，较 9 月小幅下滑 0.4 个百分点，但仍然维持在扩张区间；尽管 10
月建筑业 PMI 再度回落至收缩区间，但装备制造业 PMI 仍表现出一定韧性，我们认为，四季度扩内
需政策仍有发力空间，或对相关产业景气度有所带动。 
制造业需求整体回落至收缩区间。着眼重要细分项，10 月新订单指数 49.4%，环比下滑 1.1 个百分
点；10 月新出口订单指数环比回落 1.3 个百分点，当月值为 48.7%；10 月产成品库存指数 47.5%，
环比下滑 0.1 个百分点；制造业需求整体回落至收缩区间。 
10 月生产指数 49.8%，环比回落 1.9 个百分点；原材料库存指数48.4%，环比上升 0.2 个百分点；从
业人员指数 48.4 %，环比 9 月持平；供货商配送时间指数50.2%，环比上升 0.1 个百分点。企业预期
方面，制造业生产经营活动预期指数53.4%，环比回落 0.4 个百分点， 制造业经营预期维持在较高景
气度区间 。从生产相关的细分指标看 ， 月内在手订单和采购指数环比分别下滑 1.9 和 2.1 个百分点。 
10 月制造业生产指数环比下降，制造业采购备货、在手订单均环比下降，或在一定程度上受到假期
因素影响，国庆假期时间较长，工作日减少或对企业生产有所影响。

图表 1. 制造业 PMI 与历史同期相比（%）

资料来源：wind，中银证券

价格指数方面 ，10 月主要原材料购进价格指数 56.8 %,环比回落 4.0 百分点 ，但仍处于较高景气区间；
制造业出厂价格指数也维持在扩张区间，环比下滑 2.3 个百分点， 当月值为51.7%；主要原材料购进
价格指数水平仍然高于出厂价格指数，上游成本端或仍是 10 月价格指数的主要支撑。 
重点行业盈利能力方面，尽管 10 月建筑业景气度有所回落，但 10 月专用设备制造业长期盈利能力
领先于其他制造业子行业，四季度固定资产投资仍有望发力；同期汽车制造业短期、长期盈利能力
也在各行业中较为领先，汽车产业链盈利能力延续改善；计算机通信电子设备及仪器仪表制造业短
期盈利能力较为领先，长期盈利能力在各行业中处于中等偏上位置；此外，有色金属冶炼及压延加
工业长期盈利也在各行业中较为领先。

大型制造企业支撑继续凸显。10 月大型企业 PMI 为 50.2%，较 9 月回落 0.4 个百分点，但仍处于扩
张区间；中、小型制造企业 PMI 分别为 48.4%和 47.5%，环比分别回落 1.3 和 1.4 个百分点，中小企
业景气度仍处于收缩区间。

10 月非制造业 PMI 指数回升至扩张区间 
10 月非制造业 PMI 指数回升至扩张区间。10 月非制造业 PMI（商务活动）指数为 49.7%，环比下滑
0.5 个百分点。从需求端看，10 月新订单指数为 44.4%，环比下滑 2.1 个百分点。外需方面，新出口
订单指数为 46.5%，环比下滑 2.0 个百分点。10 月非制造业从业人员指数为 46.0%，环比上月走高
0.1 个百分点，但仍处于较深的收缩区间。供应商配送时间指数 50.7%，环比回落 0.6 个百分点。

百分点，至 49.6%；从业人员指数环比上升 0.5 个百分点，为 46.7%。

观性的其他职务，没有担任本报告评论的上市公司的董事、监事或高级管理人员；也不拥有与该上市公司有关的任何
财务权益；本报告评论的上市公司或其它第三方都没有或没有承诺向本人提供与本报告有关的任何补偿或其它利益。 
中银国际证券股份有限公司同时声明，将通过公司网站披露本公司授权公众媒体及其他机构刊载或者转发证券研究报
告有关情况。 如有投资者于未经授权的公众媒体看到或从其他机构获得本研究报告的， 请慎重使用所获得的研究报告，
以防止被误导，中银国际证券股份有限公司不对其报告理解和使用承担任何责任。 
评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达克综合指数或标普500 指数。

市場焦點
美國
• 10月S&P Global綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖較前月回落，但仍處高檔區間；服務業動
能略為放緩，製造業PMI則升至56.0，創近4年半新高。新訂單增速加快 ，顯示企業需求、投
資及庫存回補動能仍具韌性 ，然而企業售價與投入成本同步上升 ，反映需求改善 、供應鏈延
遲及成本轉嫁效應持續存在 ，通膨降溫進程仍具不確定性 。整體而言 ，市場關注焦點轉向通
膨與利率走勢，高利率環境恐維持較長時間，進而推高股債資產評價波動。
中國
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季 GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期 ，在出口與科技製造業帶動下 ，
短期成長可望維持穩定 ，但若內需未能有效改善 ，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散 ，建議聚焦在
半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈 ，及高端製造相關產業 ，至於消費 、房地產及傳統內需類股 ，

仍需觀察後續刺激政策與消費信心回升情況。
法國
政局動盪
• 法國金融市場因各地抗議活動持續擴大 ，面臨股債雙跌的局面。市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支
出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮 。法國10年期公債殖利率一度突破 5%，法德利差亦擴
大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標仍處於歷史相對低檔 ，遠低於歐
元區主權債務危機期間，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務危機的風險仍相對有限。

美國PMI維持強勁擴張，經濟成長續航但通膨壓力再升溫
• 企業需求強勁支撐美國景氣維持高檔擴張 ：美國10月綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖略低於前月，但仍處於近五年來
的高位區間，顯示企業活動持續高速擴張；其中服務業 PMI為57.9、製造業PMI升至56.0並創53個月新高，新訂單增速
更創逾4年半最佳表現，反映內需穩健、出口需求改善與企業補庫存效應同步發酵，美國第四季經濟動能仍具韌性。
• 成本上升與供應瓶頸推升通膨風險： 企業售價漲幅創2022年8月以來最高，能源、運輸、金屬原料及薪資成本同步上揚 ，
加上供應鏈交期延長與訂單積壓擴大，使企業得以持續轉嫁成本，暗示未來數月消費者通膨可能再度偏離聯準會目標區
間，市場對利率維持較高水準的預期恐因此升溫。
• 後市觀察：景氣韌性有利獲利成長，但通膨可能推升殖利率、壓抑高評價資產。投資配置建議優先布局美國大型科技股 、

美國金融股及工業資本支出受惠族群，可透過NASDAQ 100 ETF、S&P 500 ETF及美國銀行股ETF參與企業獲利成長；
固定收益則以短天期投資級債為主，以降低利率波動風險。

海外資金流入美國市場，支撐美國資產吸引力
• 海外資金持續流入美國：美國財政部8月TIC資料顯示，
整體資金淨流入 1,998億美元，其中民間部門淨流入
1,785億美元，為主要資金來源；外資淨買進美國長
期證券3,007億美元，遠高於美國投資人加碼海外資
產216億美元，反映全球資金在經濟成長與流動性考
量下，對美國金融市場仍維持高度的配置需求。
• 全球資金偏好美國資產： 外資 8月淨買入美國股票
1,319億美元，高於美國公債與公司債 ，顯示海外投
資人仍看好美國企業獲利與經濟基本面 ，雖然短期國
庫券持有量下降，部分跨境美元資金亦出現波動，但
以10月近況來看長天期公債標售仍吸引境外央行 、
保險資金及大型資產管理機構參與，反映高殖利率水
準對長期資金仍具吸引力。在經濟成長維持韌性與企

業獲利支撐下，美國資產仍為全球資金配置的重要核
心市場。
• 市場看法：海外資金流入有助反映美國資產吸引力，
惟後續仍須觀察通膨 、Fed政策及企業獲利表現 。投
資布局建議可以美國投資等級債、美國中短天期公債
及大型AI基礎建設受惠族群為核心配置 ，兼顧收益、
防禦及長期成長潛力。

歷史數據顯示全球股市突破歷史新高後，後續上漲機率高
• 很多人直覺以為見高就是頂，但過去20年的統計資料顯示，全球股市在創下新高後，多數時間選擇繼續向上走。
• 勝率顯著偏高： 全球股市 (MSCI全球指數 )創新高後持有一年 ，獲得正報酬的機率高達 62%，三個月後的正報酬機率更達
71%。美股與台股表現最亮眼，標普500指數創高後一年上漲機率為76%，那斯達克為79%；而台灣股市表現更為驚人，創
高後三個月內上漲的機率高達82%。
• 下檔拉回風險有限：在創高後短期(一個月內)修正幅度其實相當溫和 ，全球多數主要市場的回徹幅度中位數僅 1%～2%左右。
• 突破前高優於連續創新高： 若市場在歷經至少一個月以上的整理後突破前高 ，其未來一年的平均報酬率高達 8.4%，遠高於

連續每天創新高的3.9%。這代表市場經適度整理後的突破，具備更健康的上漲動能。
創新高後的平均遠期報酬率
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22
全球股市創新高後的遠期報酬率
突破前高和連續創新高後三個月

市場寬度收窄，由少數巨頭獨撐
• 當前的市場特徵由少數巨頭帶頭衝： 科技股權重較高的指數(如那斯達克、台股、韓股)，長期以來都有少數權重股拉動指數
的現象。自2024年以來，全球13個主要市場中有10個市場在創新高時，參與上漲的股票比例明顯下降 。已開發市場創新高
時高於200日均線的股票比例從過去 75.5%降至65.1%，新興市場更降至 61.3%。這意味著指數看似風光 ，但多數個股並未
同步創高，當漲勢過度集中於少數大型科技巨頭時，整體市場的抗風險能力會相對減弱。
• 小結與投資建議：歷史經驗顯示，創新高本身是強勢市場的特徵，後續繼續走揚的機率遠大於立刻崩盤。然而，鑑於目前市
場寬度收窄、權重高度集中於科技股的現狀 ，盲目追高個股的風險已顯著增加 。投資建議可以利用ETF掌握大盤趨勢，由於

指數上漲主要由龍頭股驅動 ，透過市值型ETF(如追蹤標普500ETF)能直接享受龍頭巨頭帶來的指數創高紅利 ，避免選錯個股
而賺了指數、賠了價差。此外，提防短期股市回檔，可運用定期定額分批布局或等待大盤適度拉回整理再行加碼，降低追高
風險。
創新高時不同水準的市場寬度佔比

法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓
• 法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓：法國各地抗議活動持續擴大，最初由學生發起，隨後因生活成本高漲而引發其
他群體響應。在財政空間有限、俄烏戰爭與美伊衝突推升能源價格的背景下，政府難以有效緩解民生成本壓力，法國金融市
場因此面臨股債雙跌的局面。
• 公債殖利率攀升，但系統性風險仍相對可控：市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10
年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標（CISS）仍處於
歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐元區主權債務危機期間，顯示金融市場尚未出現廣泛的系統性壓力，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務
危機的風險仍相對有限。
G7社會動盪指數，法國高居榜首

日本央行本週料維持利率不變
日本央行利率走勢預估
資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.15 資料來源： tradingeconomics，2026.10.21
日本貿易收支
• 日本貿易逆差擴大，進口增幅高於出口：日本2026年9月貿易逆差擴大至5,366億日圓，較去年同期的2,777億日圓增加93%，
高於市場預期的1,810億日圓，連續第五個月呈現貿易赤字。出口年增16.7%至10.97兆日圓，受惠於半導體相關電子零件、
製造設備及汽車出口成長；惟進口年增18.9%至11.51兆日圓，增幅高於出口，主要受到原油、半導體電子零件及液化天然
氣進口增加帶動。其中，原油進口年增51.1%，部分反映日本在荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張下，積極分散能源採購來源。
• 日本央行本週料維持利率不變：日本央行9月將政策利率調升25個基點至1.25%，創31年來新高。考量前幾次升息對經濟的

傳導效果仍待觀察，加上貿易逆差擴大反映進口成本壓力，多數分析師預期日本央行本週將暫停升息，以評估貨幣緊縮對經
濟與通膨的影響。

中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期：顯示中國經濟在第二季落底後呈現溫和回升 ，從季度表現來看，
一、二、三季增速分別為5.0%、4.3%及4.5%，反映政策支持與外需復甦開始發揮效果 ，全年達成官方4.5%成長目標的機率
明顯提升，第三季經濟改善的主要動能來自出口 ，受惠於全球企業持續擴建 AI基礎設施，及科技產品需求強勁 ，中國高科技
產品出口表現亮眼 ，尤其是積體電路 、伺服器、電腦零組件，及儲存設備等產品增幅顯著 ，高技術製造業維持雙位數成長 。
同時，中國對美國及歐盟出口維持強勁 ，推升貿易順差 走升，為支撐經濟的重要力量 。然而，內需疲弱仍是當前經濟最大隱
憂，房地產調整尚未結束，居民消費與企業投資意願保守，使經濟復甦結構呈現「外熱內冷」的特徵。

• 展望後市：在出口與科技製造業帶動下 ，短期成長可望維持穩定 ，但若內需未能有效改善 ，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散 ，建
議聚焦在半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈 ，及高端製造相關產業 ，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股 ，仍需觀察後續刺激政
策與消費信心回升情況。
資料來源：開源證券，2026.10.22

彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高
• 彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高：彭博產業研究認為，中國AI產業未來將由大型科技平台主導 ，其中騰
訊、字節跳動與阿里巴巴憑藉龐大用戶基礎 、數據資源、資金實力及完整生態系統 ，在AI競賽中具備明顯優勢 ，這些企業可將
AI廣泛應用於電商、社群、遊戲、金融與雲端業務，提升營運效率並強化競爭門檻，例如：阿里巴巴利用 AI 驅動電商平台上的
個人化產品推薦，而騰訊則運用 AI 優化短影音廣告並強化微信功能。騰訊自 2026 年 7 月起開始將混元 AI 模型整合至其電子
遊戲中，使遊戲角色及助理能更準確地回應玩家 ，然而，AI模型訓練與基礎建設投入龐大 ，獲利能力短期仍受壓抑 ，且須面對
監管、資料隱私及晶片供應等挑戰 。此外，智譜、月之暗面、MiniMax及商湯等AI新創雖具技術創新能力，但缺乏規模效益、

穩定現金流及長期資本支持，未來獲利挑戰較大。
• 投資建議：看好中國AI長期發展者，可優先關注具資金 、算力與應用場景優勢的大型平台企業 ，受惠程度可望高於多數 AI新創
公司。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.21

台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高，台股第四季有望先蹲後跳
• 台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高： 9月外銷訂單1,142.1億美元，連兩個月破千億美元 ，再創單月新高；年增率高達 62.4%，連續
20個月呈雙位數正成長 。經濟部表示，AI強勁需求超乎預期 ，預估今年第四季接單還會優於第三季 。展望10月表現，外銷
訂單將持續亮麗，估落在1,125億至1,159億美元，有望連三個月破千億美元。
• 台股第四季有望先蹲後跳： 10月初以來，台股在盤中觸及歷史高點 49,968點後面臨一波修正 ，主要反映在新一輪的財報公
布前，市場觀望AI投資是否存在泡沫化風險 。短期來看，台股在季線附近出現支撐 ，不過在11月初的美國期中選舉落幕前 ，
台股維持區間震盪的可能性較高；中期來看，美國期中選後往往因政治不確定性下降，歷史統計股市上漲機率高，加上台灣

年底集團作帳及選舉行情，正向看待年底台股表現。
資料來源：經濟部統計處，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.20。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

## Document 103: 261027_daiwa_TPCA

Source PDF: all report pdf/261027_daiwa_TPCA.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: We attended the 2026 Taiwan Printed Circuit Association (TPCA) Show that 
was held on 20-22 October. Below, we share our read-throughs from booth visits and key 
company takeaways. Overall, we remain positive on Taiwan glass fibre, CCL, PCB, and 
substrate vendors, supported by upbeat company commentary. On pricing, we expect 
low-end CCL and E-glass price increases to be larger and more sustained than previously 
anticipated. High-end CCL vendor commentary suggests plans for more meaningful price 
increases from 2H26 to maintain their products’ relative profitability amid sharp increases 
in low-end CCL prices. We also expect prices for T-glass, ABF, CCL, and substrates to 
continue rising. On the CCL technology roadmap, we favour Q-glass combined with 
hydrocarbon resin (HC) over PTFE-based alternatives as the longer-term direction. Lastly, 
we provide our view on the CCL market competition landscape change after seeing 
Nan Ya Plastics targeting the high-end market more aggressively. We also provide some of 
our key highlights from related companies within the Taiwan PCB supply chain. EMC (2383 
TT, TWD4,750, Buy [1]), ITEQ (6213 TT, TWD746, Buy [1]), Kinsus (3189 TT, TWD941, 
Buy [1]) and NYPCB (8046 TT, TWD1,255, Buy [1]) are our top ideas within our coverage.

We attended the 2026 Taiwan Printed Circuit Association (TPCA) Show last week. Below, 
we share our read-throughs from booth visits and key company takeaways.

Whether to adopt PTFE emerged as a key point of debate at the show.  
• During the TPCA show, Taiwan Union Technology (TUC; 6274 TT, TWD1,280, Buy [1]) 
unveiled its new M10 (Hydrocarbon base) and M11 (PTFE base) materials with glass-
fabric (Low-Dk2, Q-glass) and glass-fabric-free versions. Nan Ya Plastic (1303 TT, not 
rated) also unveiled its M11-grade material (ie, NPG-A99) at the show. Based on our 
understanding, NPG-A99 will continue to a hydrocarbon resin (HC) base, likely 
combined with Low-Dk2, to enhance CCL performance for high frequency and high-
speed signal transmission requirements. However, Elite Material (EMC; 2383 TT, 
TWD4,750, Buy [1]) remains reluctant to enter the PTFE-based CCL market, citing 
limited end application. Also, EMC plans to use advanced Q-glass for its M10 CCL. 
Lastly, we also heard from Panasonic’s booth representative that Panasonic (6752 JP, 
JPY3,687, Neutral [3]; covered by Ayaka Inomata) is pursuing a similar strategy for 
M9/M10 development, leveraging Q-glass instead of PTFE at this stage.

• While Nvidia (NVDA US, USD216, Outperform [2]; covered by Louis Miscioscia) 
appears keen to enhance product performance by adopting PTFE, given its very low 
dissipation factor (Df), PCB manufacturing yields remain a key concern. Accordingly, 
some leading vendors are broadening their material options (including PTFE-based 
CCL) to address potential customer requirements. EMC and Panasonic, however, 
remain firmly committed to a Q-glass-based technology roadmap. Although TUC 
highlighted progress on its PTFE-based M11 at a technology conference during the 
show, we believe it also intends to advance its existing HC resin/Low-Dk2 and HC 
resin/Q-glass roadmap for its next-generation CCL. In our view, its PTFE-based M11 
development is intended to preserve flexibility across potential technology pathways 
rather than replace its existing approach. Nan Ya Plastics’s strategy also appears 
reasonable: it can produce Low-Dk glass in-house but must source Q-glass and PTFE 
externally. This sourcing structure may explain its continued use of Low-Dk2 with HC-
resin instead of PTFE for its next-gen products, including M11.

Taiwan Data Centre Hardware

• Conclusion: which technology roadmap will prevail? We believe it is too early to 
identify a clear winner, as the supply chain continues to improve manufacturing yields 
for PTFE-based products. Some vendors remain cautious despite PTFE’s long history in 
the industry, as its thermal shrinkage and relative softness still make adoption in high-
layer-count (HLC) PCBs challenging. Given the current stance of leading global vendors 
EMC, Doosan (000150 KS), and Panasonic, together with EMC’s execution track 
record, we currently favour Q-glass-based solutions without PTFE as the more likely 
direction. Nevertheless, broadening product offerings to address different customer 
requirements should not be viewed negatively, provided vendors have sufficient 
resources to support development across multiple technology pathways.

Low-end CCL and e-glass continue to surprise on the upside.  
• During the show, we also spoke with Kingboard Laminates (1888 HK, not rated). The 
company remained positive on the E-glass outlook, although its booth representative 
provided no numerical guidance for next year following a c.200% price hike in 2026. 
Kingboard attributed the pricing strength to supply constraints, driven largely by AI 
server demand. While we highlighted increased glass yarn supply from Chinese peers in 
2Q/3Q26, the company argued that the weaving machine capacity remains the 
bottleneck. It expects to add only c.500 weaving machines in 2026 and 1.2k in 2027 (vs. 
current installed base of 3.3k). E-glass fabric price increases therefore appear 
supported by limited weaving capacity and producers shifting capacity from lower-end 
glass to higher-end products, including Low-Dk, Low-Dk2, and T-glass. Kingboard also 
noted a similar trend in low-end copper foil, where shortages have recently intensified.

• The read-through from Nan Ya Plastics is similar. The company indicated that its current 
fibreglass allocation between internal consumption and external sales is 45:55, with the 
internal share set to rise gradually as it upgrades its product mix. Its CCL portfolio 
remains concentrated in FR-4 (M2 grade), but it plans to migrate toward M4/M6, 
supported by long-term contract wins with two major hyperscalers seeking to secure 
CCL supply. In 2027, M4/M6 products are expected to account for 60-70% of its mix (4m 
out of 6.5m in capacity), with M2 and below contributing 20%. The remainder would 
comprise high-end CCL, including M7/M8/M9, reflecting recent development progress. 
We therefore expect Nan Ya’s external thin-type E-glass supply to decline as its M4/M6 
mix increases next year.

Competition from Nan Ya Plastics.   
• The current CCL shortage creates favourable conditions for Nan Ya Plastics to expand 
into the high-end CCL supply chain. Whether the company has entered AMZN’s T3/T4 
supply chain is also a key point of market debate. Based on discussions with its booth 
representatives, however, we believe management will prioritise upgrading its M4/M6 
product portfolio before pursuing the highest-end CCL market more aggressively. On the 
positive side, favourable supply-demand dynamics have supported Nan Ya’s transition 
from a low-end supplier toward the mid-to-high-end segment, although high-end CCL 
should still contribute a relatively small share of revenue in 2027. For incumbent 
suppliers, competition is clearly intensifying, but we expect the near-term impact on 
high-end peers to remain limited.

• Our read: We expect the impact on EMC and TUC to remain relatively limited in 2027. 
Competitive pressure could become more meaningful over 2028-2030, although it is too 
early to assess its magnitude.

### Page 3

Taiwan Data Centre Hardware 
27 October 2026 
3

Other key takeaways: 
• Nitto Boseki (3110 JP, JPY2,942, Neutral [3]; covered by Noritsugu Hirakawa) identified 
Taiwan Glass (1802 TT, NR) and Grace Fabric Technology (603256 CH, CNY84.95, 
Outperform [2]; covered by Dennis Ip) as key competitors. Its plan to triple capacity by 
2028 remains largely on track. The company also intends to expand capacity further by 
2030, although the details have yet to be confirmed.

• Resonac (4004 JP, JPY12,915, Outperform [2]; covered by Hidemitsu Umebayashi) 
indicated that customers are requesting ABF substrate core thicknesses of 2,000-
3,000μm, versus its current maximum of 1,600μm, to accommodate larger package 
sizes. Potential technology pathways include adopting glass cores or ceramic 
substrates, or stacking additional core layers to enhance rigidity. Based on discussions 
with booth representatives, substrate makers may move toward multi-core structures, 
which could increase T-glass consumption. However, technical challenges in enabling 
signal transmission between core layers remain a potential barrier that would need to be 
addressed over the next one to two years.

• Mitsui Kinzoku (5706 JP, JPY2,054, Outperform [2]; covered by Shinichiro Ozaki) 
indicated that, based on its technology roadmap, M10-grade CCL combined with its 
HVLP5 copper foil should support 448Gbps SerDes transmission. In the near term, the 
company plans to shift capacity from lower-end to higher-end HVLP products, with a key 
piece of production equipment scheduled for installation in 2028/29. On pricing, its booth 
representative suggested that quarterly increases of more than 10% would be 
appropriate. Regarding copper foil shortages highlighted by other supply-chain 
participants, the representative indicated that mid-tier products, such as HVLP1 and 
HVLP2, could face the most severe supply constraints.

• Unimicron (3037 TT, TWD1,060, Buy [1]) appeared to highlight multi-core structures and 
the embedding of passive components within core layers as key technology trends in 
ABF substrates.

• Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT; 4958 TT, not rated) indicated that its substrate business 
has a plan to list on the Hong Kong Stock Exchange in 1H27 under the name Leading 
Interconnect Semiconductor Technology (LIST). At the show, the company focused 
primarily on thick HDI boards and highlighted two next-generation technology trends: 1) 
sequential lamination (M+N) to improve signal integrity and increase routing density 
within the same footprint; and 2) hybrid HDI/HLC structures, which add HDI layers to 
thick HLC boards (M+N), as discussed earlier.

## Document 205: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것삼성전자(005930) 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것삼성전자(005930) 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것 - 기업.pdf

반도체업종

◼ 3분기 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 
◼ 주주환원은 시점의 문제일 뿐. 강화 방향성은 여전히 유효. 
◼ 메모리반도체 이익의 폭증이 가격의 매력을 유발. 비중확대 전략 유효.

매력적인 가격, 변하지 않은 주주환원 정책의 방향성 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 메모리반도체 주도 폭발적 이익 상승이 가격 (주
가)의 매력을 지속 유발하고 있고, 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향성에도 변한 것
은 없다는 판단. 절대적으로 싼 주가를 비중 확대의 기회로 삼을 필요. 
1. 주주환원, 시점의 문제 
금번 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 대외 불확실성이 큰 만큼, 
시장은 자사주 매입/소각과 같은 즉각적 주가 부양 정책의 발표를 기대.  
당사는 삼성전자의 주주환원이 “시점의 문제”라 생각. 총 주주환원의 규모도, 
차기 (2027-2029년) 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향에도 변한 것은 없다는 판
단. 시장이 기대하는 자사주 매입/소각은 우선주 중심으로 시행될 가능성이 
높으며, 시행 시, 그 규모는 25조원 내외일 것 (DPS 12,567원)으로 전망. 
2. 메모리반도체, 더 올라갈 것 
보수적 환율 전망 (2027년 평균 환율 1,300원 적용)에도, 2027년 메모리반도
체 영업이익은 608조원에 달할 것. 이익 전망의 상향 여력 또한 유효. 
1) 최대 생산, 최대 판매 
삼성전자는 금일 4Q26 DRAM 판매량 가이던스로 전분기 대비 10% 중후반 
성장 (q-q +18%로 추정)을 제시. 일부 투자자들은 높아진 기저 부담으로 
2027년 판매 성장이 제한될 것을 우려. 당사는 높아진 기저에도 2027년 
DRAM 판매가 전년대비 20% 상승할 것으로 전망. 
- 극단적 수급 불균형 속, 삼성전자는 “최대 생산, 최대 판매” 정책을 시행 
중. 삼성전자만의 강력한 Execution이 20% 대 성장을 유지시킬 원동력. 
- DRAM 1c 수율의 조기 개선 속, 범용 DRAM 양산에 DRAM 1c Capa가 일
부 투입될 것으로 예상. Bit 성장을 높일 수 있는 변화가 될 것. 
2) 열려 있는 가격의 상방 
1Q27 모바일 DRAM에서 Surprise가 발생할 것이라는 의견을 유지 (전분기 
대비 30% 이상 상승). 장기계약이 체결된 CSP향으로도 가격의 추가 상승 여
력이 존재한다는 점을 상기할 필요. CSP의 경우, 당장의 비용 부담보다 AI 시
장 선점을 우선시. 장기계약으로 형성된 가격 Band의 천장 (Ceiling) 또한 시
장 우려 대비 낮지 않음을 감안할 필요. 2027년 범용 DRAM ASP는 전년대비 
40% 중후반까지 상승할 가능성 (현 전망: 전년대비 34% 상승).  
3) HBM, 제품 경쟁력과 점유율의 동반 성장  
최근 삼성전자향으로 HBM4/HBM4e 추가 공급 요청이 쇄도하고 있는 것으
로 추정. 일부 고객은 연내 추가 공급 시 2027년 계약 가격을 선제 지급할 
의사를 표출. 제품 경쟁력이 회복되었음을 보여주는 증거라 생각. 
제품 경쟁력의 회복과 최대 Capa의 이점 속, 2027년은 HBM 점유율 1위를 
확보하는 기념비적 해가 될 것으로 전망 (매출액 기준 점유율 38% 예상).

산업 투자의견 
- Overweight(비중확대) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 초과 상승 예상 
- Neutral(중립) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률과 유사한 수준 예상 
- Underweight(비중축소) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 하회 예상

기업 투자의견 
- Buy(매수)  
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 상승 예상 
- Marketperform(시장수익률) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상 
- Underperform(시장수익률 하회) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 하락 예상

 직전 목표주가 530,000원  
 현재주가 (9/22) 276,500원  
 상승여력 128%

시가총액 16,164,960억원  
 총발행주식수 6,648,649,811주  
 60일 평균 거래대금 65,670억원  
 60일 평균 거래량 25,018,656주  
 52주 고/저 362,500원 / 83,300원  
 외인지분율 46.55%  
 배당수익률 1.39%  
 주요주주 삼성생명보험 외 15 인

주가수익률 (%) 1개월 3개월 12개월  
 절대 (1.8) (21.8) 231.1   
 상대 (3.2) 1.6  63.7   
 절대 (달러환산) 0.3  (11.5) 239.5

3Q26 전사 예상 영업이익 100조원 
DS부문 예상 영업이익은 100.0조원(OPM 67.4%, YoY +1538.5%, QoQ +14.5%) 으로, 
달러환율 1,380원기준, 메모리와 비메모리 사업부문 예상 영업손익은 각각 102.0조원,    
-2.0조원으로 추정한다. 1) 메모리반도체 가격 상승세가 이어지는 가운데, HBM4 판매 
본격화에 따른 영향을 고려하면 DRAM/NAND 분기 평균 가격 상승폭은 각각 +18.0%, 
+16.0%으로 추정하고, 2)비메모리반도체 사업 적자는 지속될 전망이기 때문이다. 다만 
특별 성과급 지급에 따른 충당금 영향을 제외하고 선단공정 중심의 파운드리 가동률을 
고려하면 적자폭은 유의미하게 감소한 것으로 파악된다는 점이 긍정적이다.   
SDC부문 예상 영업이익은 1.1조원(OPM 13.0%, YoY -9.6%, QoQ +54.9%)으로, 1)국내 
주력 고객사향 폴더블 스마트폰 판매 호조가 사업부의 외형 성장에 긍정적으로 작용했을 
것으로 추정됨에도 불구하고, 2) 8.6세대 IT OLED 패널 양산 개시에 따른 감가상각비(약 
0.3조원 수준)와 북미 고객 중심의 판가 인하 압박 영향으로 전년동기대비 수익성은 약 
2%pt 훼손이 불가피할 전망이다. DX부문 예상 영업손익은 -1.1조원(YoY 적자전환, QoQ 
적자지속)으로, 메모리반도체를 포함한 IT 부품 가격 부담이 전분기대비 확대되고 있기 
때문이다. MX와 VD/DA사업부 예상 손익은 각각 -0.9조원, -0.2조원으로 추정한다.

투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 63만원으로 상향 조정  
메모리반도체의 가격 상승폭이 다소 둔화되겠지만, 2027~2028년에도 타이트한 수급 
환경이 이어질 전망이라는 점을 주목한다. 1)시장은 신규 증설과 HBM 관련 노이즈로 인한 
2028년 피크아웃 가능성을 우려하고 있으나, HBM 의 범용 DRAM 생산능력 잠식과 AI 
추론 확산에 따른 고용량 메모리 수요를 감안하면 공급 제약의 해소 시점은 예상보다 
늦어질 가능성이 높기 때문이다. 뿐만 아니라, 2)메모리 업사이클이 장기화될 경우 동사 
FCF는 과거대비 구조적으로 확대되어 보다 친화적인 주주환원 정책(2027~2029년, 
2027년 1월 예정)을 제시할 가능성도 배제할 수 없기 때문이다. 동사 2027년 연간 실적 
추정치 상향 조정에 따라 목표주가를 63만원(2027년 BPS * Target PBR 3.5배 적용)으로 
상향 조정한다. 피크아웃 우려에 따른 주가 조정은 중장기 관점에서 매수 기회다.

2026.10.29   
HD현대중공업  (329180)

3Q26: 조선사는 우직해야 한다  Company Brief

3분기 매출 5조 7,536억원/영업이익 8,992억원 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 매출은 5조 7,536억원/영업이익은 8,992억원으로 
컨센서스(6조 1,295억원/9,671억원) 및 당사 추정치(6조 859억원/9,679억원) 대비 
매출 및 영업이익 각각 -6.1%/-7.0%, -5.5%/-7.1%를 하회하는 실적을 기록하였다.  
조선사업부 매출이 4조 3,988억원/영업이익 6,463억원(OPM 14.7%)이며 이중 상선 
OPM은 14.7%로, 2분기 17.8%에 비해 3.1%P 하락하였으나 1분기 15.9%와 크게 
차이나지 않는 수준을 유지하면서 조업일수 및 환율 하락에도 불구하고 P-MIX및 
빈티지 개선에 따른 효과를 보여주었다. 해양사업부는 매출 3,799억원/영업이익 
1,265억원(OPM 33.3%)로 지난 분기(15.9%)보다도 훨씬 높은 영업이익을 
기록하였다. 엔진기계사업부는 매출 9,511억원/영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)를 
기록하며 역시 견조한 실적을 기록하였다.  
일회성 이익으로 Trion 공사의 C/O 180 억원, 일회성 손실로는 셧다운에 따른 
조업감소 영향 500억원 및 환율하락에 따른 이익 감소분 450억원이 있었으며 금액은 
공개되지 않았으나 임단협 잠정타결에 따른 성과급/타결금 지급에 대한 추가 충당금의 
75% 인식이 반영되었다. 알려진 일회성만 제거하더라도 실질 영업이익률은 
17%이상으로 2분기 16.4%보다 높아진 호실적이라고 할 수 있다. 특히 RUYA 공사가 
본 궤도에 오르면서 해양사업부의 이익률이 괄목할 만큼 높아졌으며 지속가능하다는 
회사의 설명이다.

투자의견 BUY 및 조선업종 TOP PICK 유지, 목표주가 530,000원 하향 
HD현대중공업의 목표주가를 530,000원으로 38.3% 하향하지만, 투자의견 BUY와 
조선업종 TOP PICK 을 유지한다. 환율 하락 및 28년 하반기 엔진증설 매출확대, 
해양공사 이익률 상승 등 요인을 종합 반영하여 실적 추정치를 미세 반영하여 도출한 
2027F BPS 131,910원에 Target P/B를 기존대비 하향한 4.0배로 적용하였다. 특수선 
기대감 및 선가상승 기대감 하락에 따른 조선업종 전반의 밸류에이션 하락을 반영하여 
Target을 낮췄지만 그럼에도 조선업종 중 가장 높은 멀티플을 부여하며 Top 
Pick의견을 견지한다. 여러 악재에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업은 탄탄하다. 본업인 
상선사업부의 9월말 기준 수주량은 137.9억 달러로 목표치를 이미 초과 달성하였으며 
전사 기준으로도 수주목표 90.5%를 채워넣었다. 수주잔고 또한 상선 421억 
달러/전사 609억 달러로 각각 작년 말 대비 12.6%/13.2% 증가하였다. 해양부문과 
엔진기계부문은 조선사업부보다도 높은 이익률로 든든한 실적의 기반이 되고 있다.  
최근 HD현대그룹은 미국의 EPC강자 Kiewit과 미국 합작법인 설립에 관한 계약을 
맺고 그린필드 기반의 미국 신규 조선소 건설을 추진하고 있다. 빠르면 올해 중으로 
건립과 관련한 세부 사항이 공개될 예정이며 HD현대중공업이 중추적인 역할을 담당할 
것으로 보인다. 일찍부터 브라운필드 방식으로 접근한 동종사와 달리 다소 시간은 
걸렸지만 과감하게 결정된 투자다. 조선소 예상 준공 역시 2028년 이후로 예상되어 
당장 기업가치에 반영하기는 어려우나 엔진 증설과 함께 2028년 후에는 분명한 실적 
상향 요인이 될 것으로 예상한다. 우직하게 본업에 충실하며 신중하게 미래를 준비하는 
조선소, 귀감이다.

## Document 283: CPO相關設備趨勢與台廠投資機會 福邦投顧研究部202610

Source PDF: all report pdf/CPO相關設備趨勢與台廠投資機會 福邦投顧研究部202610.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-24
Pages: 30
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CPO相關設備趨勢與台廠投資機會
福邦投顧研究部
2026/10

### Page 2

2【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
結論
◼ 需求強勁
CPO 自 2027 年進入放量期，光引擎需求 2026-2028 年由 62 萬顆增至 3,500 萬顆：AI 瓶頸由算力轉向傳輸，
Pluggable 於 3.2T 世代碰到功耗與密度上限，Scale-out 交換機率先轉向 CPO，2028 年起由 Rubin Ultra NVL576
跨櫃 Scale-up 接棒。OE 占 CPO 交換機 BOM 約四成，為價值鏈核心。
◼ 關鍵瓶頸
瓶頸測試與耦合為量產瓶頸，2028 年測試設備 TAM 約 418 億元、逾七成在 Ins3/Ins4：每顆 OE 須以次微米精
度主動對光，且模組良率隨 OE 數量倍數下滑，每一關都必須篩出良品，使 OE 成本近六成落在後段。Ins2 若
跳過，2027/2028 年整體 TAM 縮減 12%/16%，但 Ins3 測試機與貼合機需求反增。
◼ 台廠機會
台廠以致茂、鴻勁最確定，萬潤受惠貼合，其餘仍屬驗證期選擇權：致茂的 Ins3 OE 測試機已取得 2027 年量產

訂單，加上 Ins4 光學測試，預估 2027 年 CPO 營收 65-75 億元；鴻勁以光電同測 Handler 切入 Ins4，預估 55-60
億元；萬潤是 FAU/receptacle 耦合貼合的主要供應商，若採 receptacle 兩段貼合，貼合機需求最高可倍增。漢測
、旺矽、光焱仍在驗證階段，能否放量取決於 Ins2 的去留與台積電 HVM 驗證結果。
◼ 看好個股
致茂 (2360)、鴻勁 (7769)、萬潤 (6187)

AI 傳輸瓶頸推動 CPO 導入，2027 年進入放量期

### Page 5

5【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
CSP資本支出今明年延續高成長，支出重心可能逐步轉向網通設備
資料來源：BBG；福邦投顧整理
2026-27年CSP資本支出續強 ，主權AI投資有望接棒支撐 2028年成長：五大CSP資本支出於2026年年增約98%，
2027年仍有望成長47%，惟隨基期墊高，2028年年增率預估放緩至11%。但觀察到近期中東、歐洲等地主權AI計
畫與Neo cloud陸續宣布更多的投資及合作 ，中國亦積極布局AI資料中心，有望拓展CSP以外的需求來源，延續
整體AI基礎建設投資動能。
記憶體成本攀升 ，AI資本支出有望逐步向網通設備傾斜： 2025年資料中心成本中 ，IT設備約占整體支出 84%，
其中伺服器約占IT設備8成，網通與存儲設備則占其餘部分 。隨記憶體價格上漲、BOM成本攀升，NVIDIA已開
始調整Rubin Ultra的HBM規格，以優化整體成本。且若單顆GPU記憶體容量受限，MoE模型參數分配、Prefill /
Decode分離及KV Cache跨節點搬移等需求，可能推升GPU間的資料傳輸量，推升高速網路的重要性。

### Page 6

6【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
AI 算 力 成 長 超 越 資 料 傳 輸 能 力， 高 速 互 聯 成 為 系 統 效 能 關 鍵
資料來源：AI and Memory Wall；Nvidia；福邦投顧整理
AI晶片算力成長快於記憶體頻寬與互聯傳輸 ，系統瓶頸由「算不夠快」轉向「資料送不夠快」： 隨前沿模型參
數規模超越單顆GPU記憶體容量，須透過張量平行等方式拆分至多顆GPU協同運算，增加跨GPU資料交換需求。
根據學界統計，過去20年硬體峰值算力約每 2年成長3倍，DRAM頻寬與互聯頻寬卻僅成長 1.6倍及1.4倍，差距持
續擴大，使GPU等待資料的時間增加，形成「I/O Wall」。
推論需求由MoE延伸至超長上下文 ，任務碎片化與資料搬移推升互聯頻寬需求： MoE專家數量增至300個以上，
加上推論任務更加碎片化，需透過GPU間all-to-all通訊，進而推升Scale-up頻寬需求。進入長上下文階段後，影音
生成與軟體開發等應用的上下文長度突破100萬個token，帶動KV cache大幅增長；在GPU記憶體容量有限及Prefill

/ Decode分離部署下，資料須跨GPU與節點搬移，將推升Scale-out頻寬需求。隨模型複雜度與資料傳輸需求持續
提升，高速互聯成為避免GPU閒置、支撐AI效能成長的關鍵，也為網通設備投資提供長期動能。
圖3、I/O牆逐步成為算力瓶頸 圖4、推論複雜度由Dense演進至MoE與超長上下文

### Page 7

7【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
Scale-out Switch逐漸轉向CPO，NVIDIA、Broadcom產品2027年放量
資料來源：SemiAnalysis；Broadcom；福邦投顧整理
功耗與密度雙重瓶頸，推動Scale-out交換機由Pluggable轉向CPO： 在功耗方面，Pluggable鏈路需經PCB走線
與DSP進行訊號傳輸及補償 ，隨頻寬提升，Insertion Loss增加，訊號處理功耗也隨之攀升； CPO將OE直接整
合於Switch ASIC周圍，使訊號離開晶片後即可轉為光，減少訊號處理環節。在密度方面，GPU數量增加帶動
Port需求成長，但Pluggable受限於前面板插槽空間；CPO則將OE配置於ASIC周圍，突破前面板的密度限制。
目前CPO應用以NVIDIA為主、Broadcom其次，Spectrum-X推出有望帶動2027年擴大部署：CPO目前主要應
用於 Scale-out交換機 。NVIDIA轉向開放式乙太網架構後 ，客戶接受度提升 ，已知客戶包括 CoreWeave、

Lambda等Neo cloud及Oracle；隨Spectrum-X於2H26推出，預期2027年將擴大部署 。相較之下 ，Broadcom的
CPO交換機目前以Meta為主要客戶，多數雲端業者仍多採用純電版本，CPO尚未大規模導入。
圖5、CPO與DSP可插拔光模組訊號路徑差異 表1、Nvidia、Broadcom CPO交換機新產品

### Page 8

8【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
Rubin Ultra將導入光互聯Scale-up，Feynman可望全面轉向CPO
資料來源：SemiAnalysis；福邦投顧整理
Scale-up轉光最快有望於Rubin Ultra NVL576落地：MoE模型架構的all-to-all通訊推動Scale-up網域由72顆GPU
擴大至576顆，需串聯8個機櫃形成單一NVLink網域。目前機櫃內仍以銅背板互連為主 ，跨機櫃則導入光互聯
，NPO（Socket）與CPO方案均在開發中。Rubin Ultra採用9+9+9的Oberon機櫃設計，中間9個Switch Tray各配
置4顆NVLink Switch ASIC，每顆ASIC搭配4顆3.2T OE，推算每櫃配置144顆OE、每組NVL576共1,152顆。
Feynman NVL1152機櫃間將全面導入CPO：下一代Feynman NVL1152將由8個Kyber機櫃組成，機櫃間同樣採
用CPO；NVIDIA Roadmap亦已明確規劃Feynman將採用NVLink 8，並於機櫃間互聯全面導入CPO，機櫃內則

可能由銅互聯轉為CPO。

### Page 9

9【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
隨CPO / NPO Switch 逐步放量，OE需求有望於2026–2028年快速攀升
資料來源：Nvidia、台積電、廣發、福邦投顧整理預估
台廠Scale-out為2026–2028年CPO主要應用 ，2027年OE需求有望年增逾 18倍：以三層 Fat-Tree架構及 Spectrum
6800/6810為基礎推估，假設NVIDIA於2026/2027/2028年的CPO滲透率分別為10%/30%/50%，Scale-out CPO交換
機出貨量約為 4.8/91.8/154.7k台，對應OE需求約616/11,748/19,800k顆，2027/2028年年增率分別約 1,807%/69%。
預估2026–2027年CPO OE需求將主要來自Scale-out應用。
Scale-up初期以NPO導入，帶動2028年OE總需求上看3,500萬顆： 以Rubin Ultra NVL576每櫃配置144顆OE估算
，並考量良率及維修性需求，假設Scale-up初期採用NPO方案。若2027/2028年Rubin Ultra採用NVL576的比例分

別為 30%/50% ， 對應 Scale-up NPO OE 需求約 720/15,200k 顆 。 綜合 Scale-out CPO 與 Scale-up NPO 需求 ，
2026/2027/2028年OE總需求約為 616/12,468/35,000k顆，2027/2028年年增率分別約 1,924%/181%；其中CPO占比
將由100%降至94.2%及56.6%，顯示隨Scale-up自2028年起放量，NPO OE需求占比將明顯提升。

### Page 10

台積電主導 CPO 封裝，測試與耦合成量產瓶頸

### Page 11

11【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
Scale-out先行CPO，Scale-up因良率可能先採NPO過渡
圖8、從光模組到CPO的封裝工藝路線圖，過渡階段可能先以NPO的形式來做Scale-up
資料來源：TSMC、福邦投顧整理
Scale-out CPO現階段採OE on Substrate封裝：依台積電光互連技術路徑，目前Scale-out CPO交換機主要採OE
on Substrate架構，將OE與Switch ASIC共同封裝，尚未進展至將OE整合於Interposer的OOI階段。待封裝良率改
善後，OE有望進一步移至Interposer，相較現行CPO仍有約2.5倍的能效提升空間，為長期發展方向。
Scale-up初期可能先以Socket式NPO為主，以效能換取良率與維修性：NVL576跨櫃互聯提供CPO與NPO兩種方
案，CPO將OE直接封裝並外接雷射光源，NPO則透過Connector以Socket形式安裝OE，可單獨更換。CPO單一封
裝整合數十顆OE，任一顆失效即可能整顆報廢，良率隨OE數量增加而下降；NPO雖因多經Substrate走線與連接

器而效能略遜，但可避開此風險。

台積電COUPE拿下NVIDIA與Broadcom，CPO平台領先同業
圖9、各家矽光、封裝平台整理
資料來源：台積電、穎崴、福邦投顧整理
台積電以SoIC-X混合鍵合拿下NVIDIA與Broadcom兩大CPO客戶：SoIC-X將EIC直接鍵合於PIC上方，大幅降低
寄生效應，使NVIDIA得以實現200G/λ MRM，平台亦同時支援Broadcom的MZM路線。加上GC耦合支援晶圓級測
試與量產、完整PDK元件庫，以及由CPO延伸至OOI的路線圖，形成其他平台短期難以複製的整體方案 ，為目前
最佳的CPO量產平台。
新材料世代仍為其他平台留下切入機會：Broadcom陣營同步採用GF與Tower分散供應風險；若400G/λ世代須轉向
TFLN或InP等新材料，平台優勢可能重新洗牌。

### Page 13

13【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
台積電COUPE兼具GC與EC耦合，兼顧量產測試與低損耗
圖10、台積電COUPE-GC與COUPE-EC耦合架構
資料來源：台積電、福邦投顧整理
GC垂直耦合可做晶圓級測試 ，為COUPE率先量產的方案：COUPE-GC在PIC上製作光柵耦合器 ，光訊號垂直進
出，經Si Carrier上的Si lens聚焦並擴大對位公差，下方金屬反射層（MR）再回收散失的光以降低損耗。由於光從
晶圓表面進出，切割前即可逐顆量測，可直接銜接Insertion 1晶圓級測試。
EC邊緣耦合損耗更低，但須切割後才能對光：COUPE-EC由晶片側邊Facet進光，以SiN Tip轉換光模態進入波導
，損耗較低且對波長 、偏振較不敏感，利於往更高頻寬與 DWDM發展；惟須切割後才能耦光 ，Facet品質與對位
精度要求更高，測試須延後至Insertion 3。

### Page 14

14【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
光引擎佔CPO交換機價值40–50%，為最高價值零組件
圖11、CPO交換機價值鏈：OE 佔整機價值 40–50%
資料來源：GS、福邦投顧整理
OE為CPO交換機中價值最高的零組件 ，約占BOM成本4成：根據GS預估CPO交換機成本拆解來看，以NVIDIA
Quantum-X 交換機為例，總BOM成本約7.58萬美元，其中72顆光引擎合計約3.24萬美元，占BOM約43%，為4顆
Switch ASIC（占16%）的2.7倍；顯示CPO交換機的價值核心已由交換晶片轉向光學系統 。OE由PIC與EIC經
SoIC混合鍵合而成，負責電光轉換、調變與驅動，取代傳統可插拔光模組的核心功能；製程上須歷經 PIC與EIC
晶圓製造、混合鍵合、切割與receptacle耦光貼合，期間並穿插多道晶圓級與晶粒級光電測試 ，製程複雜度與附
加價值皆集中於OE。

### Page 15

15【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
OE成本近6成在封測，測試佔SiPh元件成本75%
圖12、OE成本結構、與SiPh元件製造成本拆解
資料來源：智璞、福邦投顧整理
OE成本近6成來自封裝與測試，晶圓製造僅約4成：以單顆OE約100美元拆解，PIC、EIC晶圓與SoIC堆疊
分別約占11%、17%、14%，合計約42%，其餘59%來自封裝與測試；若PIC或耦光良率偏低，報廢晶片將
推升晶圓端占比。
SiPh測試成本占比約75%，遠高於InP元件的29%：每顆die須先完成光纖對位才能量測，加上PDK未成熟
、多在切單後逐顆測試；若在晶圓階段完成KGD測試，占比可由封裝後的90%降至約60%，具晶圓級光測
與高精度對位能力的設備廠最受惠。

### Page 16

16【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
CPO封裝過程需經4道主要測試，以決定CPO量產良率
圖13、CPO光引擎4道測試流程與設備供應商
資料來源：富果、TrendForce、MoneyDJ、福邦投顧整理
CPO需額外加入光學測試，測試複雜度大幅提高：光電混合元件除電性外，還須驗證光訊號，凡涉及PIC的
測試都須先將光精準耦合進晶片，且OE封裝後難以重工，因此須在晶圓、Hybrid bonding、OE切割及模組組
裝完成等節點確保每顆OE皆為良品，由於光耦合對位耗時，量產瓶頸正由「對得準」轉向「測得快」。
CPO良率會隨OE數量呈倍數下滑： 一顆Switch ASIC周圍需搭載30顆以上的OE，假設單顆OE良率為95%，
以32顆計算，整顆CPO模組良率僅約19%；若單顆OE良率提升至99%，模組良率才能回升至約72%。任一顆
OE失效都可能導致整組模組報廢，因此必須在每道製程前先篩除不良品。

Insertion 1：量產測試確定導入，測試時間有望大幅縮減
資料來源：Advantest、致茂、光焱、汎銓、TrendForce 、福邦投顧預估整理
PIC耦光對位為測試瓶頸 ，漏光預篩可望成為提速關鍵： Ins1於晶圓階段對EIC與PIC進行CP測試，EIC沿用一
般電性Probing，PIC則需在Prober加裝光學對位模組，量測光路損耗與MRM、PD等元件特性。由於每顆die都
須以次微米精度重新對準光纖，測試耗時成為量產瓶頸。業界正導入漏光定位設備作為預篩，先剔除漏光或有
潛在缺陷的die，免跑完整量測，以縮短整片晶圓的總測試時間 ；其中光焱NightJar 單格掃描不到1秒，量產版
NightJar-H正於台積電進行HVM驗證。
台廠方面：光焱可望受惠於預篩設備導入，致茂則以PIC整機方案送驗中，若通過驗證可望切入Insertion 1。
圖14、PIC檢測機台示意圖 圖15、PIC/EIC晶圓測試重點整理
項目
測試內容 EIC：同一般電性ProbingPIC：Prober加裝光學對位模組，打光量測損耗

核心目的 檢測光路損耗（耦合器、波導）及MRM、濾波元件、PD等元件特性
核心技術 6軸平台夾持光纖探針、可調雷射＋光功率計、波長掃描找微環共振；選配紅外線漏光定位
主要瓶頸 每顆die需重新對準光纖，次微米級閉環耦合，耗時過長
關鍵供應鏈 測試機：Teradyne、Advantest；探針台/卡：TEL、FormFactor；6軸平台：PI；光學量測：Keysight
市場現況 先以漏光定位篩出潛在缺陷die，PIC測試由100秒/die縮至<5秒/die

### Page 18

18【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
Insertion 2：技術尚未成熟仍待定，去留成觀察重點
資料來源：Advantest、Teradyne、旺矽、致茂、光焱、漢測、TrendForce ；福邦投顧整理
Ins2為CPO測試最難一站，雙面架構拖慢產出：Ins2於EIC以SoIC混合鍵合至PIC晶圓後、切割前進行測試，以上
電下光方式量測光迴路 、電光轉換之間的各種參數 。由於測試價值隨階段往後遞增 ，提前剔除不良die可提升後
續封裝良率，並劃分晶圓廠與OSAT之間的良率責任。此站需一面電性接觸、一面光耦合並同步精準對位 ，並兼
顧EIC發熱對Micro-Ring的影響 ，雙面架構難以多site平行測試，現行方案多為單site，單片晶圓測試約需6小時以
上，因耗時過長部分業者傾向暫時跳過，2027年放量看法分歧。
台廠方面：Advantest + 旺矽持續出二代機驗證中，漢測主要幫ficonTEC代工，光焱則提供快篩替代方案。
圖16、EIC/PIC D2W晶粒示意圖 圖17、EIC+PIC鍵合後晶圓測試重點整理

項目
測試內容 EIC以SoIC鍵合至PIC晶圓後、切割前，上電下光測E/O、O/E、O/O、光迴路及S參數
核心目的 測試價值隨階段遞增，提前剔除不良die以提升後續封裝良率
核心技術 雙面探測（一面電性接觸、一面光耦合）並同步對位；晶粒級溫控，避免EIC發熱影響微環共振
主要瓶頸 難以多site平行測試，多為單site、產出低；光耦合易受震動、溫度與翹曲影響
關鍵供應鏈 Teradyne＋ficonTEC（第二版驗證中）；Advantest＋旺矽（工程機已出貨，量產驗證中）
市場現況 單片晶圓測試約6小時以上；部分業者傾向暫時跳過，2027年放量看法分歧
台廠受惠 旺矽（Prober＋Probe card）、漢測（ficonTEC代工）、光焱（快篩替代方案）

Insertion 3：Die Level全檢不可省，Shift to middle成CPO測試長期主力
資料來源：致茂、穎崴、萬潤、漢測、TrendForce；福邦投顧整理
Insertion 3為Die Level全檢，單顆取放拉長測試時間： Insertion 3於OE切割並貼合receptacle後進行測試，量測
光路品質（IL、PDL）與loopback誤碼率（BER），目的是避免切割、貼合過程產生的不良OE流入後續載板黏
合製程。相較Insertion 2的Wafer Level測試，Insertion 3須單顆取放、溫控與對接並全數檢測，未來可能每顆OE
的測試時間約為Insertion 2 per die的2–3倍。量產前全檢為必要條件，部分流程已導入大功率與老化測試，測試
時間仍有上升空間，但未來可能往多site平行測試發展以提升產出。
台廠方面：漢測（ficonTEC驗證中）、致茂(Tester出量產機)，萬潤則為FAU / receptacle耦合貼合主要供應商。

Insertion 4：Switch ASIC+OE成品測試，SLT為最後一塊拼圖

（加測回送誤碼率 ），以及在接近實際運作狀態下跑真實流量的 SLT。預計未來將加入光電同測 、SLT，目前
Ins4eo連測試參數都尚未完全確定 。測試難點在於CPO元件尺寸大、功耗高，FAU設計不統一，散熱頭與光學對
接存在空間衝突，且ASIC與OE需雙區溫控；SLT更需供應大量外部雷射光源，測試複雜度仍高。
台廠方面：FT純電、Ins4eo以Advantest/Teradyne搭配鴻勁分選機為主，Ins4o致茂(tester)。

Known Good OE的最後關卡；Insertion 4則須驗證整顆CPO封裝的光電功能，並逐步導入SLT，單顆測試價值最高

### Page 23

23【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
Ins2 不做可以節省成本，且隨 SoIC 製程良率提升測試時間減少
圖24、每產出一顆良品OE所需測試時間比較
資料來源：福邦投顧整理預估
SoIC 良率越高，不做 Ins2 每顆良品 OE 所需測試時間越少：不做 Ins 2 可省下每顆 die 在 Ins 2 的 45 秒，代價是
SoIC 不良品會額外占用 Ins 3 的測試時間，因此節省幅度取決於 SoIC 良率。良率約 60% 時兩者打平；良率越高
、不良品越少，每產出一顆良品 OE 所需的測試時間由良率 60% 時的 187.5 秒降至 80%/90% 時的 140.6/125.0 秒
，相較有做 Ins 2 分別節省約 28/38 秒（-17%/-23%）。
整體測試設備TAM縮減，但 Ins3 機台需求增加：若省略 Ins2，SoIC 後的良品與不良品 OE 都將直接進入 Ins3 受
測，受測量放大為 1÷SoIC 良率倍，Ins3 因受測 OE 增加，所需機台數也將大幅提升。但因省下整段 Ins2 機台，

整體而言 2027/2028 年測試設備新增 TAM 仍將分別減少約 12%/16%，可為客戶省下更多設備投資成本。

25【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
省略Ins2推升貼合機需求，2027年貼合機TAM增加約24%
圖26、FAU貼合位置示意圖
資料來源：TSMC、福邦投顧整理預估
FAU為光纖與PIC之間的耦合介面 ，現行有兩種貼合設計： FAU貼合於光引擎側邊 ，負責將光纖與PIC耦合
。現行設計分為兩種：先貼 receptacle再安裝FAU，以及直接將FAU貼合於OE上；前者可能需要兩道貼合製
程，後者僅需一次。
省略Ins2後不良品也須貼合 ，2027年貼合機需求增加約24%：以直接貼合FAU估算，有做Insertion 2時，僅
SoIC後的良品OE需要貼合；若省略Insertion 2，未經篩選的不良品也會一併進入貼合製程，使2027年貼合機
TAM增加約24%。隨SoIC良率提升、不良品減少，兩者差距逐步收斂 ，2028年已大致相當 。這也代表在量
產初期良率仍低時，省略Insertion 2的成本不只是測試，也會反映在貼合機台與貼合材料的額外消耗上。

Receptacle設計若需兩段貼合，2028年貼合機TAM有望達100億以上
資料來源： 萬潤、高明鐵、惠特；福邦投顧整理預估
Receptacle 設計有望使貼合機 TAM 最高倍增：以每次貼合約 3 分鐘估算，直接貼合 FAU 於 2027/2028 年新
增貼合機 TAM 約 43/51 億元；receptacle 設計須經 receptacle 貼合與 FAU 安裝兩段製程，若兩段皆需同等時間
的主動對位，TAM 最高可增至 85/101 億元。
台廠萬潤為主要受惠者，高明鐵、惠特送樣中：萬潤為目前最主要的 FAU 耦合貼合設備供應商，已出機至矽
品；若最終採 receptacle 兩段貼合設計，貼合機需求最高可倍增，萬潤作為現行主要供應商受惠幅度最大。其
他台廠方面，高明鐵、惠特目前均在送樣階段，國際競爭者則包括 MRSI 及港建代理的 ADST。

## Document 322: Indonesia_ Policy mix aligns (2026-10-12)

### Page 1

SHIFT IN NARRATIVE

After a rough first half of the year, the 
narrative around Indonesia’s economy has 
turned relatively constructive . The macro 
outlook is on the mend, and policy coherence 
has strengthened. Recent budget 
developments have reduced the likelihood of an 
outright fiscal slippage. A more disciplined 
fiscal trajectory should also provide room for 
monetary policy to stay focused on its price 
stability mandate rather than on ad dressing 
financial stability risks due to fiscal concerns.

On the markets front, e ven as external triggers 
impacted the rupiah, the one -sided sharp 
depreciation run in the currency in the first half 
of the year has stalled . Debt markets have  
attracted offshore interest, alongside strong 
demand for shorter-tenor SRBI securities, all of 
which have contributed to the overall capital 
flows picture.

Ahead of MSCI’s final take on the domestic 
equity market’s classification in November, 
however, equity markets  find little favour . 
Nonetheless, material improvement s in the 
stock listing s and regulatory requirements 
undertaken by the stock market and financial 
regulators are expected to contribute to the 
medium-term stability of the capital markets.

Key domestic risks that plagued the economy 
in the first half of the year have been alleviated 
to some extent, while a few other monitorables 
remain.

www.dbs.com/insightsdirect/macro groupresc@dbs.com 
• Indonesia's macro narrative has 
turned more constructive in 
2H26. 
• Key domestic policy concerns 
have eased. 
• Policy alignment has improved. 
• Greater fiscal discipline should 
allow BI to remain focused on 
price stability.  
• The current account is likely to 
widen as the trade surplus 
narrows. 
• Portfolio and FDI inflows should, 
however, narrow the BOP 
shortfall. 
• Few monitorables remain, 
including factors in the 2027 
fiscal math. 
• IDR performance will remain 
heavily influenced by global 
conditions.

Radhika Rao, Senior Economist 
radhikarao@dbs.com

### Page 2

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

NEAR-TERM FISCAL WORRIES 
RECEDE

Fiscal fears have moderated in the near 
term. New Finance Minister Suahasil 
Nazara reaffirmed the government’s 
commitment to maintain the annual 
deficit below -3% of GDP threshold. The 
2026 run-rate suggests that the deficit is 
likely to close within the budgeted target.

By September 2026, the fiscal deficit 
stood at 1.2% of GDP , reflecting a 
normalisation in spending and firm 
revenue growth. Total spending rose by 
a still -strong 1 9% yoy , even though the 
pace moderated from 1H, backed by 
disbursements towards the flagship free 
food program, subsidy and 
compensation outlays , as well as 
personnel expenditure.  Realised 
expenditure stood at 69% of the full -
year budget by 9M26.

On the other hand, revenues rose sharply 
by 25 .5% yoy (partly benefiting from 
base effects as readings were negative 
at the same time last year), helped by 
higher tax collections, export duties, and 
non-tax receipts. Revenues have already 
accounted for 74% of the budgeted 
scale by 9M26, suggesting full -year 
target is likely to be met.

Global crude prices are a key risk for the 
fiscal outlook, as prevailing Brent prices 
are above $100bl at the time of writing 
(vs budgeted ICP at $70bl), which will 
feed through higher subsidy and 
compensation outgoes, as authorities 
remain hesitant to raise fuel pump prices.

Significant efficiency measures have, 
however, been put in place  to contain 
spending, including reductions in 
outlays towards flagship schemes , 
which will help contain the extent of 
deterioration in the fiscal math. For 
instance, the flagship MBG program, 
which started with an allocation of IDR 
35trn, was  moderated to IDR 268trn 
(realised spending by Sep26 was IDR 
153trn). Successive stimulus packages

### Page 3

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

have been announced but the scale has 
been contained.

2027 math signals fiscal prudence, soft 
spots warrant attention

The official goalposts for 2027 point to 
a continued preference for fiscal 
prudence rather than fiscal activism.

Spending is on course to surpass the 
symbolic IDR 4000tn level, ris ing nearly 
4% yoy, with outlays divided between 
the centre and regional governments. 
The mix points to steady allocations 
towards subsidies, social transfers , and 
capital expenditure,  with ministry -level 
spending (K/L) set to contract, while non-
K/L spending is budgeted to increase. 
More notably, spending is expected to 
rise on key flagship programs – for 
instance, social protection (up 10%), the 
largest component education (up 14%) , 
which includes the free meals program 
at IDR 240trn.

On the revenue end, state revenues are 
expected to rise by 9% yoy compared to 
the budgeted 2026 APBN/ 7% vs the 
revised 2026 outlook. Compared to 
baseline 2026, tax revenues are 
expected to rise 10.5%, besides higher 
non-tax collections. This math lands the 
2027 budgeted deficit at -2.4% of GDP 
vs revised 2026 at -2.85% of GDP.

Few uncertainties that loom.  Firstly, 
next year’s math rests on an upbeat 
growth projection of 6 .0% (DBSf 5.2%) , 
besides modest depreciation in the 
rupiah and about  a 7% increase in the 
Indonesia crude price assumption (see 
table). Some of these expectations 
might be challenged as the year unfolds. 
Next, state revenues are expected to 
rise to a n upbeat  12.5% of GDP , likely 
riding on a strong growth forecast, 
better tax due diligence , and improved 
administration via the Coretax 
mechanism.

Thirdly, crude prices are a key risk to the 
fiscal math. Given the economy’s 
position as a net oil importer, various 
official commentaries put the sensitivity  
at every $1pb oil worsening the fiscal 
balance by IDR 6trn, after accounting for 
oil-related revenues. This implies that for 
every $10pb increase, the deficit could 
be impacted by ~IDR 60trn, not far from 
MOF’s sensitivity of IDR 68trn (~0.25 -
0.30% of GDP) due to higher subsidy and 
compensation costs  (ICT assumed at 
$75bl vs prevailing Brent at $100bl). 
Spending will need to reprioritised to 
accommodate additional allocation 
towards subsidies and compensation.

### Page 4

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Next, the direction of travel on the 
contribution of SOE dividends from the 
investment agency to the state coffers 
for 2027 remains unclear, after being 
accounted for this year. Lastly, 
successive stimulus measures in-kind 
and wage subsidies were announced this 
year and could marginally add to the 
strain in 2027 (0.1-0.2% of GDP).

Discussions around the State Finance 
Law also warrant a watch. We maintain 
our deficit forecast at a wider -2.8% to -
2.9% range to account for any revenue 
shortfall or a gradual pace of growth. 
Nonetheless, deficits/debt are likely to 
stay below key thresholds  and not 
emerge as a flashpoint for the markets.

MONETARY POLICY ON AN EVEN 
KEEL

Bank Indonesia has left the benchmark 
rate unchanged  since July 2026, after 
100bp hikes in 1H26. While tighter global 
financial conditions and geopolitical 
risks were key risks on the horizon, the BI 
held the domestic GDP forecast at 4.9 -
5.7% (DBSf: 5.3%) alongside 
manageable inflation, notwithstanding 
El Nino-related risks. Inflation rose to 3.3% 
yoy in Sep26, towards the high end of the 
target range. Authorities have, however, 
assured that the impact of higher food 
prices due to poor weather will be 
addressed through administrative 
measures by regional bodies.

Contained inflation expectations ha ve 
reduced the urgency for BI to tighten 
policy further, allowing policymakers to 
prioritise growth and financial stability 
considerations. The emphasis on 
strengthening the balance of payments 
position reflects continued caution over 
external financing conditions and capital 
flows. However, with the rupiah 
expected to remain stable on the back of 
policy measures and fundamenta ls, the 
BI did not view current external 
pressures as severe enough to warrant 
additional tightening.

BI introduced a host of measures at the 
June to September policy meetings , 
including FX monetary hedging 
instruments and a mechanism to provide 
foreign investors with greater flexibility 
in managing IDR-denominated 
investments in Indonesia.  
These included –  
a) swap hedging per tenor bucket was 
increased to 3M - 15%; 6M - 20%; 12M - 
25%, from 12.5% earlier.  
b) DNDF incentive was increased to 6M - 
25% and 12M – 30%. An increase in FX/ 
NDF swap premium incentives for longer 
tenors will lower hedging costs and

### Page 5

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

encourage longer tenor positions, in turn 
increasing longevity of these flows;  
c) Launch of the VASTRA scheme (FX 
Market Transactions for Hedging 
through Partner Banks) got underway, 
with the naming of 14 domestic partner 
banks. This facility is designed to help 
investors transact with lower underlying 
documentation requirements, tap into 
longer banking hours , and access 
onshore hedging facilities.  
d) deepen FX and money markets by 
expanding Local Currency Transactions 
(LCT) between IDR and CNY , besides a 
few other currencies.

Policymakers are likely to tap a 
combination of administrative and 
macroprudential measures rather than 
solely interest rates to defend the 
currency as well as keep inflationary 
expectations in check.

We expect the pause on rates to extend 
into 4Q26.  This call might be at risk if 
depreciation pressures on the rupiah 
mount again on unfavourable global 
developments and if the US Fed 
undertakes a more aggressive hik ing 
cycle than our baseline (of 75bp hikes).

WIDER CAD WILL BE PARTLY 
OFFSET BY POSITIVE FLOWS

Indonesia’s 1H26 current account 
deficit (CAD) widened significantly  to -
$16bn vs -$3bn in the comparable period 
a year ago. Much of this deterioration 
was led by a narrower goods trade 
balance, while  the services deficit 
widened on the seasonal travel account 
due to the Hajj period. Higher coupon 
payments weighed on the primary 
account.  This pressure is likely to persist 
in 2H as the oil & gas trade deficit 
remains wide and export performance 
has been fi rm but tracking lower than 
regional peers, likely also impacted by 
the El Nino phenomenon in key agri 
commodities. We expect full year CAD to 
be closer to 1.8-2% of GDP from -0.1% in 
2025.

Much rests on the capital flows account. 
While 1H balance of payments (BOP) also 
widened, there was palpable 
improvement in 2Q26 vs 1Q.  2Q BOP 
narrowed significantly to $0.9bn from a 
wide $9.1bn in 1Q, helped by positive 
capital account flows – primarily 
stronger portfolio investment on SRBI 
and debt inflows, and net FDI position. 
This dependency is likely to extend to 2H, 
with capital fl ows till Sep26 holding up 
on account of hedging discounts and 
moves to improve inflows. Global 
conditions, including the narrow ID -US 
yields, however, warrant attention. 
Nonetheless, full-year BOP could remain

### Page 6

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

wide, on a scale comparable to the 2025 
gap.

IN SUMMARY

IDR markets have been relatively 
steady in October despite higher oil 
prices and a bid dollar , though 
sentiment remains sensitive to external 
developments and swings in global risk 
appetite. USDIDR traded within 17600 -
18000, while 10Y yield hovered around 
7.15-7.25%. A jump in the Aug goods 
trade surplus aided this respite, 
alongside foreign purchases of domestic 
debt, which have remained positive this 
month and on a  YTD basis. Insurers, the 
central bank, and retail investors have 
remained key anchors for the bond 
market. SRBI rates are off highs to sub -
7% levels but are attracting steady 
demand, likely encouraged by hedging 
discounts. An improved outlook for flows 
has helped contain spillovers from a 
wider current account deficit. This does 
not take away from the fact that the 
rupiah is amongst the worst performers 
on a year-to-date basis.

Separately, markets will also monitor 
developments in the political economy, 
institutional architecture, and formation, 
as well as the role of state -led agencies 
(e.g., DSI) and progress on the 
centralisation of decision -making. We 
expect the BI to keep on hold, however, 
pressure to tighten policy might return if 
the US Fed hikes rates well into 2027 
and the US dollar benefits from a risk-off 
environment, weighing on the currency.

Great improvement in 3Q26

Intel reports 3Q26 sales of US$16.5bn, 2% qoq and 21% yoy, in line
with Bloomberg estimates. 3Q26 gross margin of 43% was 2ppts
higher than Bloomberg estimates. 3Q26 EPS of US$0.6 is much
higher than Bloomberg’s US$0.28 largely due to 2ppts better gross
margin, 4ppts better operating margin and non-operating income of
US$1.1bn.
Data center IC sales beat: 3Q26 Data center IC sales (largely
server CPU sales) were US$7.32bn, up 17% qoq and 78% yoy,
nearly 10% above consensus estimates. Data center IC sales

accounted for 44% of 3Q26 sales, up from 2Q26’s 39% and from
3Q25’s 30%. Operating margin for data center IC sales was 42%,
much higher than Bloomberg’s 27%, and improved from 2Q26’s
40% and 3Q25’s 23%.
Improve in progress for foundry business: In addition to
stronger data center IC sales, Intel foundry 3Q26 sales improved to
US$6.7bn, up 16% qoq and 58% yoy. Driven by higher utilization
rate, 3Q26 operating margin for Intel foundry was improved to -28%

### Page 2

from 2Q26’s -36% and 3Q25’s -55%, which was much better than
Bloomberg’s -36% estimates.
Source: Intel, Bloomberg est.

### Page 3

Source: Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
4Q26 guidance missing on sales, margin, and EPS
Combined eﬀects from lack of demand on client computing (due to
memory price hike and PC customers to aggressively manage
inventory might extend to 1H27) and lack of data center CPU
capacity (limited by in house capacity after Israel Kiryat Gat fab
factory shut down), Intel guides 4Q26 sales of US$15-16bn, down 3-
9% qoq and 9% below Bloomberg estimate of US$17bn (Fab shut

down aﬀects 2ppts sales). 4Q26 gross margin is guided to
deteriorate to 37% (Fab shut down aﬀects 2ppts margin), 6ppts
lower than 3Q26’s 43% and Bloomberg’s 42.8%, resulting much
lower diluted EPS of US$0.1 (Bloomberg’s US$0.3).

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable, 
the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
Markets have had little respite over the past month. Hopes 
of a lasting US–Iran agreement have faded and intermittent 
attacks on shipping have kept Brent oil prices in triple digits. 
Workarounds have allowed a meaningful share of crude to 
continue flowing but these arrangements are slower and 
costlier, while depleted inventories leave little room to 
absorb another shock and a range of disruptions to refining 
and shipping are also impacting downstream energy costs. 
Bond markets have been just as restless, with continued lifts 
in government bond yields across much of the globe and 
more sharply in the US. 
Notably, oil is no longer doing the heavy lifting for yields. 
The rise has been concentrated in real yields, with the Fed’s 
September hike – and its signal of more to come – acting as 
something of a trigger. In the US, fiscal concerns have been 
more of a sub-plot, though the Treasury’s efforts to hold 
down long yields have arguably only drawn more attention 
to them. 
In our view, markets are getting ahead of themselves on 
monetary policy offshore. We expect just one more hike 
from the Fed rather than the three the market currently has 
priced, with stretched housing affordability posing risks to 
both consumption and inflation. Central banks in Europe, the 
UK and Canada are also likely to bide their time after a final 
move. Europe is weathering the energy shock better than 
feared and has far more fiscal room to respond than the US – 
one reason we still see the US dollar trending lower over the 
next two years.
The RBA hiked again in September and looks set to follow 
up in November, taking the cash rate to a new cycle high. 
Some of the risks the Board flagged have crystallised, 
but the minutes also reveal a tendency to reach for the 
more hawkish read of the data, in addition to the familiar 
downbeat take on supply capacity. Our base case assumes 
no return to full-scale conflict, but with energy buffers 
depleted, bond markets less forgiving and the RBA already 
leaning hawkish, the margin for error – for markets and 
policymakers alike – is starting to ‘wear thin’.
Australia: After a soft but better than expected first half, the 
Australian economy is facing into more challenges as another 
round of disruptions from global energy markets combine with 
further interest rate rises and a deepening housing downturn. 
The demand mix should see capacity pressures continue to 
ease but with significant risks on either side.
Middle East: Low inventories and higher shipping costs will 
keep oil and fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast 
to average US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by 
Q4 2027 as supply recovers. Risks are two-sided: escalation 
could push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran 
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply.
Commodities: Most commodities rose in September, with 
many of the themes that drove moves across August remaining 
in place. Japanese LNG and Brent oil recorded strong gains 
amid Middle East tensions. Copper and aluminium extended 
recent gains while iron ore was supported by higher freight 
and production costs. Gold fell after the Fed’s September rate 
hike. 
Global FX Markets: In late September and early-October 
the DXY index popped higher to near 103, a high back to 
early-2025. Underlying the move are expectations that 
inflation will prove persistent globally as well as a belief that 
the US’ tech sector leaves it best positioned to weather the 
storm.
New Zealand: Higher fuel costs and a weaker exchange rate 
have lifted the short-term inflation outlook while the economy 
has continued to grow through the Iran war. Growth looks set 
to continue to be buoyed by strong commodity prices and a 
supportive exchange rate. However, downside risks are growing 
from higher energy costs and long-term interest rates. 
United States: The US housing market remains under 
considerable pressure. Prices continue to edge up, supporting 
wealth. But up-and-coming main street consumers are 
struggling with affordability and availability, posing enduring 
risks for both consumption growth and inflation.   
China: With each month that passes, the risk of disappointing 
authorities’ 4.5-5.0% ambition grows. While the Government 
is right to take a targeted approach to support, it must quickly 
multiply its scale if downside risks are to be kept at bay. 
Asia: For much of the past twenty years, Asia has been a 
source of disinflation for the world. But a combination of ageing 
populations, rising labour costs, increasing electricity demand 
and technology sector developments imply Asia may provide 
less support to global disinflation over the decade ahead.
Europe & UK: The euro has come under considerable pressure 
during the past month. That said, the European economy 
continues to tick along nicely while inflation risks remain 
manageable, allowing the ECB to be measured in their response.

Wearing thin

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
The main development affecting global markets this month
has been the sharp lift in selected bond yields, especially in
the US and France. Since the previous Market Outlook was
published, 10-year benchmarks have risen 27bps for US

Treasuries, to be well above the 5% threshold, and 40bps for
French bonds. The latter move contrasts with German bunds,
which have seen little change in yields at that tenor over the
month, though they are up more than 60bps year to date.
Several factors are behind the latest rise, with no single
cause dominating. For some of these factors, the underlying
conditions had been in evidence for some time, but recent
events focused attention on them. The rise has been primarily

a story of real yields: longer-run inflation expectations implied
by both breakevens and swaps have not increased that much.
For much of the year, fluctuations in oil prices – and thus the
near-term inflation outlook – fed into market views of central
bank policy, and longer yields as well. More recently, though,
this correlation has broken down. Shifts in expectations about
central bank policy have remained important for bond pricing,
as evident in the concentration of the increase in real yields.

The FOMC’s decision to increase the Fed funds rate, and flag in
the ‘dot plots’ that at least one more hike was likely, seems to
have been something of a trigger event for repricing, not only
for US yields. Also salient were the various actions taken by
the US Treasury to suppress long yields and improve the US
government’s debt-servicing position. These efforts had little
lasting effect and yields are now higher, and higher relative to
peers, than before. But they may have served to draw attention

to the growing issues around US fiscal sustainability. High
supply of debt from hyperscaler firms to fund the AI / data
centre boom has also likely pressured yields at the margin.
That said, fiscal concerns are unlikely to have been the main
issue driving the lift in US yields. The slope of the US yield
curve is noticeably flatter than at the beginning of the year;
in other words, short yields have risen even more than the
10-year yield, signifying tighter expected monetary policy

lifting yields across the curve. Another lens on this shift is that
2-year and other short-dated yields have increased more than
estimates of risk-neutral rate, such as that from the NY Fed.
Fiscal concerns have been more salient in the case of France,
where spreads to bunds have widened from around 70bps at
the beginning of the year to nearly 140bps currently, wider than
Greece or Italy, with about 40bps of the widening occurring in
the past month. Contagion to other euro area sovereigns has

been limited, with spreads widening around 22bps over the
month for Greece, Italy and Belgium, and 13bps for Spain.
Many factors lifting global bond yields ...
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
... and RBA policy rates outlook
In Australia, the RBA hiked the cash rate again at its late
September meeting, and a further hike at the November
meeting looks likely. This would take the cash rate to 4.85%, a
new cycle high.
While the forecasts the RBA published in August did not

support further rate hikes, in September these forecasts were
characterised as resting on several things going right and
certain risks not emerging. Some of these risks, including a re-
escalation of the war in the Middle East and thus energy prices,
have since crystallised. The post-meeting communication
and minutes also pointed to the July CPI result being above
expectations, as was June quarter GDP growth “at the margin”.
The RBA is also wary about pressure on both construction

costs and retail prices of IT goods arising from the AI/data
centre boom. No weight is put on the possibility that this new
investment and technology might add to productivity or the
capital stock, and so supply capacity, even with a lag. Indeed,
the post-meeting minutes highlighted the financial stability
risks if valuations in this area should disappoint “even if the
technology were ultimately successful”, as if the latter was not
yet clear. This language points to a generally downbeat view of

Australian supply capacity that also emerges in its discussion
of productivity trends. Although its position piece in the August
2025 Statement on Monetary Policy  indicated that lower trend
growth in productivity had no implications for inflation or
monetary policy, more recent statements imply that it does.
More broadly, the minutes for the September meeting revealed
a tendency to contemplate more hawkish interpretations
of data and outcomes. For example, the minutes dismissed

evidence of the labour market easing a bit more than
was forecast by pointing to the changes to the survey
arrangements. At the same time, the minutes contemplated
that the rate of unemployment consistent with inflation
being at target might have risen, even though wages growth
has marginally undershot the RBA’s forecasts and the cost
pressures cited by its business liaison contacts are mostly
non-labour costs. The minutes also included an extended

discussion of the possibility that the global neutral interest
rate might have increased recently. If true, this would imply
that financial conditions were less restrictive. This conclusion
would be hard to square with yield curves that have flattened
since the beginning of the year and other central bank
estimates of the term premium having increased, both of which
suggest tighter financial conditions.
That said, the RBA will find it hard to look past inflation

outcomes in the second half of year that is likely to remain
sticky. We anticipate trimmed mean inflation to record
quarterly outcomes of around 0.9%qtr in Q3 and Q4, remaining
around the current 3½%yr pulse. The Monetary Policy Board
will want to see clear evidence of inflation coming down before
withdrawing policy restrictiveness, so we continue to pencil in
August as the likely date for start of the rate-cutting phase.

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN ECONOMY
After posting a soft but better than expected first half, the
Australian economy is facing into more challenges near term
as another round of disruptions from global energy markets
combine with a further rise in interest rates and a deepening

housing downturn. Against this, business investment is in
the midst of a sustained ramp-up led by a large and growing
pipeline of data centre and energy investment projects.
Elsewhere, public demand is growing at a slower pace and
new dwelling investment is likely to moderate but both are
expected to still see positive growth. Overall, the demand
mix should see capacity pressures continue to ease but with
significant risks on either side.
Shifting political winds

Some of these risks are now in the wider policy space. The
political landscape has changed materially since the 2025
Federal Election with opinion polls showing a sustained swing
against the Labor government and a marked rise in support
for One Nation that raises the prospect of a three-horse race
in the next election, the outcome of which could be very hard
to predict (we may get a preview of this with next month’s
Victorian state election). While the next Federal election is

still a long way off – not due until well into 2028 – the shift
does appear to be having some bearing on policy. For example,
the government’s choice not to reinstate the temporary
halving in fuel excise tax that was deployed when fuel prices
first spiked back in March suggests it is less inclined towards
providing broad based cost-of-living support that might make
it harder to bring inflation back down.
Migration policy is shaping more clearly as a battleground.

A suite of changes announced by the government in mid-
September and which came into effect in October, are already
expected to slow working holidaymaker and foreign student
arrivals, moderating total net overseas migration from
around 300k current to 225k by 2028, trimming population
growth from around 1.4%yr to 1.2%yr. For its part, the main
Liberal-National opposition party has proposed to reduce net
migration to 100k in the first two years of government, before

increasing to 130k and 160k in the two years following –
which would likely see total population growth slow to around
0.7-0.8%yr initially. One Nation has proposed a period of ‘net
negative’ migration, lowering net migration by 250k a year
over the first three years of government before considering a
‘ceiling’ of 130k per year thereafter. This would see population
growth slow to around 0.2-0.3%yr initially.
While none of these policies are on the table for this year

or next, the direction of travel is clear with a risk that the
current government moves further towards curbing migrant
More challenges near term ...
Matthew Hassan, Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting
inflows. Slower population growth basically maps one-to-
one to GDP growth although the specific flow-on impacts to
areas like housing and labour markets are more complex and
will depend on the population mix and how the demand and
supply effects balance out. For the record, we have trimmed

our own population growth forecasts in the 2028 out-year,
from 1.4%yr to 1.2%yr with a related tweak to our GDP
growth forecast for that year.
Consumer sentiment-spending gap
Near term challenges continue to centre on the consumer.
Here, the latest updates show sentiment again plumbing
historical lows but more mixed messages around spending
which is still holding up better than might be expected based
on a simple read on the consumer mood.
Certainly that mood remains deeply negative. The Westpac–

Melbourne Institute Consumer Sentiment Index dropped
4.7% to 80.4 in October, putting the month amongst the forty
worst reads since the began in the early 1970s, following
two others reads in this group back in April and June and
another eight recorded in 2022–23. Altogether this marks the
worst period of recurring extremely weak sentiment since the
disastrous recession in the early 1990s.
This has been difficult to square with the soft rather than

outright weak picture on consumer spending through most
of this year. Even when real spending was recording more
material per capita declines back in 2023-24, these were
somewhat milder than what might have been expected given
the weakness in sentiment at the time.
We explored this more fully in our August Red Book . Some
of the disconnect may be a by-product of high inflation which
‘hits different’ sentiment-wise. Cost of living pressures,

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN ECONOMY
... centred on uneven consumer, housing downturn ...
which are the central issue for consumers at the moment,
represent a broad-based but shallower squeeze rather than
the deeper shocks that typically come when big economic
downturns hit. Labour markets appear to be an important

point of distinction – despite the weakness in sentiment,
consumers have been relatively unperturbed about the jobs
outlook in recent years. Adding consumers’ unemployment
expectations to sentiment measures significantly improves its
‘fit’ as a predictor of per capita spending.
There other aspect that helps to reconcile the sentiment-
spending gap is the detailed picture across segments. There
may be one economy but there is not a single consumer.

Instead both sentiment and spending outcomes reflect the
average across a range of often quite varied conditions in
different consumer segments.
This shows through in the dispersion of sentiment across
sub-groups. The chart right shows the current distribution of
index reads across the 100-odd sub-groups we regularly track
with some selected groups called out. If the range seems wide
its because it is – measures of spread over the last two years
have been 50-60% wider than the average over the previous

three decades. Exactly why is not completely clear but the
observations point to a more fractured consumer with multiple
fault lines – including across states, metro/regional areas, voter
groups, labour force status, income levels and housing tenures.
Spending-wise, one of the clearest emerging fault lines is across
age groups. Here, there look to be three main ‘blocs’ – 18-34 year
olds (mainly millennials’); 35-54 year olds (mainly ‘gen X’); and

those aged over 55 (dominated by the ‘baby boomers’).
Demographically, the 55+ age group is both the largest and
the fastest-growing – our estimates suggest it now makes up
about 45% of all households, up 8.5pts compared to 2020. It
is also seeing significant financial shifts with the cohort coming
through carrying materially larger superannuation holdings
(reflecting a variety of factors including a longer period of
compulsory contributions that have earned compounding

returns) and more housing wealth. Again, our rough estimates
suggest average household wealth in this group is around
$2.5m up over $800k since 2020 (reflecting both the
aforementioned demographic shifts and valuation changes).
At the same time, the group has low debt levels – it is largely
untouched by interest rate increases and probably benefits
slightly insofar as this lifts interest income both directly and
indirectly via super holdings.
For 18-34 year olds, incomes are more important than balance

sheets, meaning inflation poses more of a constraint. For 35-
54 year olds, income is less of a constraint but high debt levels
poses a big challenge as rate rises increase servicing costs.
All of which is to say that it should come as little surprise that
spending by the 55+ age group has been running stronger
than the other age groups. Breakdowns from our Westpac
Consumer Panel show average non fuel spend has been rising
at a 5% annual pace over the last six months amongst those

aged 55+. That compares to a more sedate 2.7-2.9% pace 
across the other age groups, which given the current pace of 
inflation, implies small declines in real, inflation-adjusted terms.
These demographic contrasts also account for the observed 
strength in key bellwether spending categories like new 
vehicle purchases. The total number of new car purchases 
by consumers is up close to 4%yr, led by strong growth in 
EVs. Notably, industry figures suggest the purchase of used 
cars is down sharply, by close to 9%yr (used car sales are 
not well covered in the statistics and largely bypass official 
ABS measures because the are mainly transfers between 
households rather than a new addition). 
Pulling these clues together, at least some of the resilience in 
spending looks to be a form of ‘wealth effect’ where balance 
sheet considerations are more important than the real purchasing 
power of incomes. While the impact of employment, wages, 
inflation and interest rate changes will still likely be the dominant 
driver of shifts in spending growth, the nature of this wealth 
effect and how it evolves from here could be a key swing factor. 
Housing downturn deepens
And where that swing factor may start to turn is in response 
to a deepening downturn in Australia’s housing market. The 
combined impact of interest rate rises and major changes to 
housing-related tax policy announced in the Federal budget is 
continuing to weigh heavily. We have been flagging for some 
time that uncertainty could see a more significant ‘air pocket’ 
emerge with very thin trading conditions and a sharper move 
lower in prices. That risk is crystallising with turnover already 
down 20% and prices off 8%. 
With the September interest rate rise and an expected hike 
in November still to come, more weakness is likely near term. 
We revised our house price forecasts lower with a decline of 
around 8% now expected for calendar 2026, with a peak-to-
trough decline of just over 10%. 
... wealth effects an uncertain swing factor 
This constitutes a more material housing downturn. The 
peak to trough fall is set to be biggest since the beginning of 
the national price measures in 1980, although prices in real, 
inflation-adjusted terms recorded larger falls during the early-
1990s and early-1980s recessions with capital city measures 
also suggesting there were larger real price falls during the 
recessions in the mid-1970s and early 1960s. 
It also points to a bigger housing-related drag on growth. 
As discussed previously, the main channels are through 
new dwelling investment, turnover and wealth effects. New 
dwelling investment is still carrying solid momentum (the 
number of dwelling under construction up 10% over the year 
to June) but is expected to see a slightly more pronounced 
slowing. Turnover is also expected to be slightly weaker. 
However, it is the wealth effects from price declines that is the 
area to watch. Our rough estimates suggest this will account 
for most of the –0.8ppts on annual GDP growth over the 
year to June 2027. The emphasis here though is on ‘rough’. 
Wealth effects have seeming disappeared for long periods 
and their precise timing is very unclear, e.g. how should the 
drag from the current downturn be viewed against what 
were presumably strong positives coming off the much larger 
preceding gains? Some of this could be due to ‘assymmetric’ 
behaviour, with effects playing out very differently when there 
wealth is rising or falling.
On balance, we expect the impact to see consumer spending 
‘muddle through’ rather than fall back to the weaker growth 
seen during the more intense cost of living and interest 
rate shocks in 2023-24. This partly reflects the indirect 
support coming from growth more generally – the AI-related 
investment boom, which we have covered at length in previous 
reports, a key factor helping to hold GDP growth up around 
1.5%yr and prevent a more material weakening in labour 
market conditions. For the consumer though this will continue 
to feel like a struggle with growth flat in per capita terms 
and a shakier picture around housing that will play out very 
differently for some segments.
